**D-331 — Integrated tag-substrate ARCHITECTURE: tags = the load-bearing SSoT substrate · ONE shared fact-producer · CI = the drift-gate · plugin = the surface — one producer, N consumers — 2026-07-06 (operator, re-deriving the endstate).** The operator articulated the whole thing as one cohesive system: the TAGS are the substrate every tool reads; the TOOLCHAIN is ONE fact-producer (recordlayout/sizeprobe/asm/clangd — no Class-18 mirror, D-310/321); the CI is auto-rederive + drift-gate (the D-320 cache-gate GENERALIZED to all axes); the plugin is the nvim surface. **Linchpin: all consumers read the ONE producer + the ONE substrate → integration is STRUCTURAL (guaranteed by the single core), not hand-aligned.** Every feature idea this session (compare-view, tag-enriched trees, the card) fell OUT of it as "another consumer," confirming the shape. D-327 completes "CI checks everything": codegen facts are CI-checkable when [BUILD]/[INSTANTIATION]-pinned (pinned→written→diffable) → the gate covers everything writable, the plugin previews everything live. **D-332 — STOP the conversion; COMPLETE THE SUBSTRATE FIRST — the format was proven on ONE comment-light file + is materially incomplete (the premature-substrate catch) — 2026-07-06 (operator, from EngineCommon + the reassessment).** D-330's "immediate full P3 conversion" premise ("design done, rest is mechanical") is FALSIFIED. RegimeDetector was ONE comment-light consumer; the CoreFrameworks corpus (EngineCommon file-header discipline blocks, annotated includes, registry row/column docs, helper-decls, dense step-comments) carries shapes the v1 schema cannot hold losslessly. Converting now would COMPRESS that detail away + lock in an incomplete format → re-traverse every file when it grows. **Three operator disciplines fire:** don't-generalize-substrate-before-input-space-known · design-once-maintain-forever · complete-not-incremental. ⇒ complete the format + toolchain + plugin BEFORE converting — NOT scope-creep, catching a premature generalization before it costs the codebase twice. Captured: north-star § 2. Sisters: feedback_dont_generalize_substrate_before_input_space_known, D-330 (superseded framing), D-333.

**D-333 — LOSSLESS-FORMAT requirement: the schema must grow to hold EVERY comment shape (SUB-unit + SUPER-unit + STRUCTURED-prose reach) — the 3-survey input-space taxonomy — 2026-07-06.** Three comment-shape surveys (A Strategies+ML · B CoreFrameworks · C primitives/parsers/backtest/GUI; ~50 gaps) → the META-FINDING: the v1 schema is UNIT-granularity + flat-prose; real docs need SUB-unit (registry [ROW]/[COLUMN], struct field-clusters), SUPER-unit (cross-fn lifecycle, cross-file [PHASE]), and STRUCTURED prose (labeled [COMMENT], [FORMULA], numbered). Highest-leverage: [ROW]/[COLUMN] sub-schema · labeled [COMMENT] · generalized [SECTION] (===/----/nesting) · widened [REFERENCE] "prefix-zoo" · wire/persist completeness (ordinals+EXCLUDED+CSV/JSON/FIX) · [FORMULA] (CI-exempt) · [DIAGRAM] sub-types+Unicode. Captured: format-input-space-taxonomy.md (the schema-completion spec). Gated on the 7 open format decisions (D-fmt-1..7). Sisters: D-322 (vocab granularity), D-332.

**D-334 — PLUGIN REWORK requirements — the unit CARD + the HUD/PANEL role-split + the graph-browser — 2026-07-06 (operator, across the session).** The plugin's ~40 features reorganize around ONE frame: the unit **CARD** = the universal renderer (file:lines · [TYPE] · [tags] · [DERIVED] · [OVERVIEW]; collapsed=one-line-char-budgeted / expanded=the HUD; polymorphic over [TYPE]). **HUD vs PANEL = two roles, NOT 1:1:** HUD = transient single-card auto-follow (quick check); PANEL = persistent MULTI-card board, EXPLICIT-add (`<leader>dD` ADDS the current unit; **ACCUMULATE-not-replace** — the current auto-rotate-and-close is a bug), `L`=open-in-panel-beside. Navigation = re-rooting (follow an edge → that card becomes root) + back/forward history. Also: cursor-tracking via [TYPE]↔[END_TYPE] · tag-enriched + tag-FILTERABLE dependency trees (file:line → [TYPE Name]+tags; "only SLOW_PATH consumers") · dual-panel COMPARE (two cards + shared edges) · context-gated actions (D-328) · collapsed-by-default · responsive layout · pop-out doc-viewer · m/M keybind · better module grouping. Captured: north-star § 6. Sisters: D-328, D-330, tasks #10/17.

**D-335 — The system FRAME + tool inventory: an in-house IDE = the tag substrate + FOUR tool roles (AUTHOR/SEE/NAVIGATE/VERIFY) + an emerging fifth; the code becomes a QUERYABLE graph — 2026-07-06 (operator: "the whole cohesive thing").** Frame: tags = substrate; every tool is an AUTHOR (writes tags), SEER (compiled reality), NAVIGATOR (browses the tag graph — the card is its renderer), or VERIFIER (CI). Once every unit is tagged with typed relationships, the codebase IS a queryable graph (tags=schema, edges=foreign-keys) → every tool is a VIEW over it (HUD="neighborhood" · tag-filter=refinement · CI="drifted?" · doc-viewer="referenced doc"). **Inventory by status (north-star § 7.5):** ✅ HUD/panel/byte-map/clangd/validators/cache-gate; 🟡 compiled-reality probes (RC-A)/overlays/derived-write/action-menu/scaffold-gen; ⬜ :FoxTagUpdate/complete-schema/unit-card/tag-filterable-trees/graph-browser/compare/doc-viewer/tag-query/generalized-drift-gate/code-tag-index; ⬜ "5th role" (un-built): registry-row browser · orphan detector · version-timeline · [SEAM]/parity view · [DEFERRED] queue · [REFERENCE] resolver. Every ⬜ is a view over the one graph, not a new engine. Sisters: D-331, D-334.

**D-336 — Toolchain root-causes (asm + overlay audits) + the GO-FORWARD SEQUENCE — 2026-07-06.** Two adversarial audits mapped the 🟡 toolchain to 5 root-cause groups: **RC-A** (THE SPINE — header/template TUs instantiate nothing → 0 instr / no layout / clangd omits Size / sizeprobe compile-errors on template params; fix = inject a concrete instantiation anchor; resurrects ~7 features) · **RC-B** (compile-DB GUI-only+stale) · **RC-C** (SIMD detector wants AVX, engine is scalar-xmm; SWAR asm-undetectable) · **RC-D** (facts.derived struct branch unwired + clang/g++ codegen mismatch) · **RC-E SAFETY** (branchtag false-greens "✓ branchless" on 0-instruction headers — dangerous on a branchless-discipline engine). **GO-FORWARD SEQUENCE (supersedes D-330's immediate-conversion):** the 7 format decisions → complete the schema → dogfood corpus → toolchain fixes (RC-A..E + :FoxTagUpdate + generalized drift-gate) → plugin rework (D-334) → THEN convert. Homed: tasks #8-19. Sisters: D-330/332/334, tasks #9(RC-A)/15(RC-B)/16(RC-E).

**D-fmt-1..7 — OPEN FORMAT DECISIONS (pending operator; GATE the schema-completion) — 2026-07-06.** From the taxonomy synthesis: (1) inline version-tags (899×) → code-local + plugin-derived-nav [rec] vs hand micro-tag; (2) canonical bars → `====` unit / `----` section, normalize `===`/Unicode to ASCII [rec]; (3) [DIAGRAM] Unicode → allow inside [DIAGRAM] (swim-lanes/trees) [rec]; (4) [SECTION] vs Phase → [SECTION] local, [REFERENCE]_[PHASE] cross-file [rec]; (5) SWAR → source-idiom detection vs manual tag; (6) commented-out-code → [FUTURE_WORK] vs delete; (7) [FORMULA] → CI-exempt from the derivable-fact check [rec]. Captured: taxonomy § Open decisions. Sisters: D-333.

**D-337 — CENTRAL STANDARDIZED TOOLCHAIN: consolidate the fact-producers + tag-parsers into ONE core (C++ or Rust) with bindings, consumed by CI + plugin + CLI — D-331's "one producer" realized as ONE codebase — 2026-07-06 (operator).** Today the fact-producers are SPLIT: Lua (plugin — recordlayout/sizeprobe/asmexplorer) + Python (CI — check_cache_layout/check_code_tag_blocks) + a headless-Lua bridge (emit_record_layout.lua) — a latent Class-18 seam (two impls that CAN drift; the exact thing the system fights). Operator's proposal: a CENTRAL toolchain core (C or C++, Python bindings; custom tag-parsers, potentially ripgrep-based fast-scan) that ALL consumers call — CI via the Python binding, the nvim plugin via subprocess/FFI, a CLI. Makes D-331's "one producer, N consumers" STRUCTURAL at the code level (ONE binary, not N reimplementations) — the ultimate anti-Class-18, and the mechanism that INTEGRATES the recent plugin ideas (card / graph-browser / doc-viewer) with the earlier toolchain/CI: all become thin clients of the same core. **Shape:** tag parser + fact-producer orchestrator (drives clang/g++) + a query engine. **Bindings:** Python (CI) · subprocess/FFI (nvim plugin) · CLI. **Sequencing:** emerges DURING the toolchain-fix phase (phase 3) — build RC-A..E fixes + `:FoxTagUpdate` INTO the core, not scattered scripts. **Sub-decision (OPEN):** language — C++ (engine-stack fit + FoxLIB reuse, pybind11, CLI for nvim) vs Rust (ripgrep ecosystem, memory-safe, PyO3); lean C++ unless the ripgrep pull wins. Captured: north-star (toolchain layer). Sisters: D-331 (the architecture), D-310/321 (one fact-core), D-336 (phase 3).

<!-- D/C/F: D-338 -->
**D-338 — VERSION/PROVENANCE IDENTIFIER standardization: the ship-version scheme is verbose + AMBIGUOUS + unparseable → decision 1's version-timeline needs a canonical grammar (going-forward; history immutable per H21) — 2026-07-06 (operator-raised).** Problem: the deeply-nested ship identifiers (`v5.15.5.F.4d.1.E.1.A`) are variable-depth + not descriptive, and a bare dev/ship "Phase H"/"Phase 8" (the #3 of the Phase-overload — dev provenance, NOT a section) is AMBIGUOUS: the same letter recurs across version branches (which ship's Phase H?). For **D-fmt-1** (version-tags stay code-local + the plugin DERIVES a version-timeline) to work, the identifiers must be PARSEABLE + SORTABLE + unambiguous — they are none of the three today. **⚠ H21 CONSTRAINT (Knight Capital):** existing version identifiers are historical-record + append-only IMMUTABLE — you CANNOT rename `v5.15.5.F...` in old comments/persisted state. So the rework is SCOPED going-forward only: (a) a canonical version-tag GRAMMAR (a defined, sortable version segment + a descriptor — e.g. `[EDIT]_[v5.15.5.F]_[lazy rebuild predicate]`; the 899× tags already carry the descriptor informally); (b) OPTIONAL a VERSION REGISTRY (one canonical doc defining each ship + its phases → a bare "Phase H" resolves version-scoped; a tag can `[REFERENCE]` it — kills the multi-"Phase H" ambiguity); (c) the timeline tool reads (a). The dev/ship "Phase H" is a VERSION identifier (this grammar), NOT a `[SECTION]`. **OPEN sub-decision:** the exact grammar (operator input — structure vs terseness) + registry yes/no. In scope but SCOPED (standardize forward, never touch history). Sisters: D-fmt-1 (version-tags), H21 (identifier immutability), D-337 (the core parses it).

**D-fmt SLATE RESOLVED + D-338 — the 8 format decisions LOCKED — 2026-07-06 (operator).** Gate lifted; schema-completion (phase 1) can start. **1** (version-tags) → code-local, verbatim in `[CODE]`; plugin derives the timeline from the D-338 grammar. **2** (bars) → ASCII, 3 weights (`====` unit / `~~~~` mid / `----` section). **3** (diagrams) → **ASCII-UML** (`+--+` boxes · `|`/`---` links · `>`/`v` arrows), NOT Unicode + a **diagram-helper tool**. **4** (`[SECTION]` vs Phase) → plain `[SECTION]_[Phase N]` label; `[REFERENCE]_[PHASE]` **DROPPED** (the dep tools own the code-cascade; a grep of the label owns the concept-grouping); cascade-viz → D-334 (feed CODE_MAP/DAG/dep-trace into the plugin trees). **5** (SWAR) → `[SWAR]` is a **SUB-TAG of bit-packing** (`[BITPACK]_[SWAR]`), auto-detected from `BITMAP_*`/`MBS_*`. **6** (commented-out/stale) → `[FUTURE_WORK]` (scaffolds) + **`[OUTDATED_INFO]`** (stale → CI flags → **human deletes**, not auto). **7** (`[FORMULA]`) → sub-kind **`[DIAGRAM]_[formula]`**, CI-exempt, plugin-displayable. **D-338** (version grammar) → canonical parseable/sortable grammar GOING FORWARD + optional registry; **NO history wipe** (H21 on persisted versions; dev-provenance preserved; a clean public `v1.0.0` is a SEPARATE release-semver call if wanted). **New schema primitives surfaced:** sub-tags · `[OUTDATED_INFO]` · the version grammar · the diagram-helper + cascade-viz tools. Sisters: D-fmt-1..7, D-338, D-333 (the taxonomy this feeds), D-334 (cascade-viz).

<!-- D/C/F: D-339 -->
**D-339 — UNIVERSAL UNIT SKELETON + a kind→`[DERIVED]`-axes TABLE: one block shape for every `[TYPE]`; the ONLY per-type variance is a DECLARATIVE axis-table, never a parser branch → no per-type adapters. `[ROW]`/`[COLUMN]` grammar LANDED as the registry instantiation — 2026-07-06 (operator).** The operator pushed the unification one level past the format: *"a standardized format that gets parsed the same way across every type… so we don't need separate adapters between struct/function/registry."* Confirmed GROUNDED — `check_code_tag_blocks.py` has ZERO `if TYPE ==` special-casing (one innermost-bracket rule, category-set spec-derived; its parse rule is *"exactly as the plugin's tagadapter"* = the two impls D-337 consolidates). **Resolution:** there is ONE unit skeleton every `[TYPE]` fills; the ONLY thing that varies by type is which facts the fact-producer emits into `[DERIVED]` — and that variance is a **declarative table** (STRUCT→SIZE/ALIGN/STRADDLE · FUNCTION→SIZE(instr)/SIMD/BRANCHES/UPSTREAM · REGISTRY→ROW_COUNT/ENROLLED/CONSUMERS · FILE→CONTAINS/INCLUDES/BLAST_RADIUS), never a code branch. Adding a unit type = one table row, not an adapter — the anti-Class-18 discipline (D-331 one-producer / D-337 one-core) realized at the grammar level: one skeleton, one parser, N renderers. **`[ROW]`/`[COLUMN]` grammar (the registry instantiation; survey gap #1):** grounded in the real `FOREACH_GLOBAL_CFG_FIELD` (a 13-column positional X-macro whose `/* === Group (n) === */` dividers already carry the trailing `\`). (a) `[COLUMN]_[name]_[meaning]` legend lines in the ORIENT region, **listing order = tuple ordinal** (position IS meaning in a positional macro — no explicit numbering; the operator's "same as how a struct lists its fields"); enumerated columns append a token/bit-set; validator asserts COLUMN-count == tuple-arity. (b) `[ROW]_[id]_[why]` **SPARSE**, addressed by the row's identity column, + `[ROW]_[TOMBSTONE]_[id]` (H21). (c) **group dividers = the universal `[SECTION]`, NOT a registry-special tag** — the in-macro `/* === … === */` stays VERBATIM in `[CODE]` and the plugin DERIVES the row-group TOC from it (no mirrored `[SECTION]` list — anti-Class-18). **The `\`-continuation crux DISSOLVED:** annotations live OUTSIDE the macro (COLUMN above `[CODE]`, ROW below `[END_CODE]`) → nothing new is ever injected between backslash-continued rows; `[CODE]` stays byte-verbatim (D-326). Landed: schema § "One skeleton" + kind→axes table + `[ROW]`/`[COLUMN]` grammar grown from stub + Registry worked example (`FOREACH_GLOBAL_CFG_FIELD`). This is **item 1 of the 9 schema-completion additions** (NEXT: labeled `[COMMENT]` · concurrency block · `[ASSERT]` · wire/persist completeness · widened `[REFERENCE]` · numeric-domain · ASCII-bar fix · D-338 version grammar · then the `[SCHEMA]` v2 bump). Sisters: D-331 (one producer N consumers), D-337 (one core), D-333 (gap #1 of the taxonomy), D-326 (code-local verbatim), D-fmt-4 (`[SECTION]`), Class-18.

**D-340 — Full-file-dogfood folds + the unified cross-type MEMBER MODEL (3 tiers), reconciling D-309 `[REGION]` + D-339 `[ROW]`/`[COLUMN]`; `[REGION]` un-under-framed — 2026-07-14 (operator-delegated on the end-goal criterion: "whatever serves the plugin end-goal + the standardized format we've designed").** The full-file dogfood on `CfgFieldRegistry.hpp` surfaced 3 ripe folds (nested units · `[ASSERT]` static-assert-as-doc · the constexpr `[SIZE]_[N/A_FOLDED]` marker); then Caramel's *"did I forget context?"* caught that **D-309** (2026-07-05, 7 grammar-gap dispositions "awaiting operator review") had ALREADY adopted **`[REGION]` as THE sub-unit binding** — the member level this session was re-deriving as `[FIELD]`/`[SECTION]`-bands. **RECONCILED into a unified cross-type MEMBER MODEL** (the D-339 sibling): individual fields/statements stay inline (D-326); a structured part sits at one of THREE tiers, same across every `[TYPE]`, tier-2 vocab per-type — **(1) grouping** `[SECTION]` (TOC divider, no own facts) · **(2) fact-scoping member** `[REGION]` (struct — byte-range/bit-slot scoping own `[THREAD]`/`[SYNC]`/`[RESIDENCY]`/`[BIT_OCCUPANCY]`, D-309 #1) / `[ROW]`+`[COLUMN]` (registry, D-339) / `[VALUE]` (enum, PROPOSED pending the `[ENUM]` dry-run + fence-add) · **(3) nested sub-unit** promoted to its own block via `[CONTAINS]` (the nested-units fold). Promotion between tiers = the § Coverage proportionality bar; the SAME member-grammar (`[KIND]_[id]_[why]`, sparse, id-addressed, the D-339 parse rule) runs at every tier → **no per-type member adapters**. **Each tier IS a plugin render** (tier-1 → TOC/jump-list · `[REGION]` → byte-map regions overlay / `regions[]` in facts.lua · `[ROW]`/`[COLUMN]` → registry-tree · tier-3 → cursor-track innermost) — the render hierarchy the plugin walks; serves BOTH end-goals (plugin nav + one-scheme format), which is the operator's ratify criterion. **Fixed:** the schema's `[SECTION]`/`[REGION]` bullet under-framed `[REGION]` as a bare `[SECTION]` grouping → split into `[SECTION]` (tier-1) + `[REGION]` (tier-2, D-309 #1 richness restored). `[ASSERT]` (LIGHT unit, `[ASSERT]_[FAMILY]_[expr]` + `[WHY]`; families `{LAYOUT_LOCK·BITMAP_OVERFLOW·EPOCH_TRIPWIRE·OVERLAP_EXCLUSION}`; assert ENFORCES / `[SIZE]` DERIVED REPORTS) + the constexpr `N/A_FOLDED` DERIVED disposition stay as landed (constexpr marker will UNIFY with D-309 refinement B's unit-level `[LAT_EXEMPT]` at the next increment). **STILL-OPEN — adopt-as-D-309-disposed (next increments):** the 4 axes `[COMPLEXITY]`/`[APPLY_AFTER]`/`[MUTATES]`/`[WIRE_VERSION]` + refinement A (stale-DERIVED-DROPPED, not relocated) + refinement B (unit `[LAT_EXEMPT]` ⋃ the constexpr marker) + clarifications a/b/c + the `[ENUM]`/`[TYPE]` dry-runs (D-309 "still needed before conversion"). Landed: schema § "The member model — three tiers" + `[SECTION]`/`[REGION]` split + the increment-2 fold grammar (nested-units · `[ASSERT]` · `N/A_FOLDED` · `[ASSERT]` variant entry · CfgFieldDescriptor worked example). Sisters: D-309 (the pre-break dispositions this reconciles), D-339 (the node model this mirrors), D-326 (inline-verbatim), D-315 (`[FILE]` `[CONTAINS]`), D-334 (plugin renders), D-332 (don't-generalize — why `[VALUE]`/edges wait for the dry-runs).

Landed: fence += `VALUE`; member-model `[VALUE]` row confirmed (SPARSE + wire-CODE); `[ASSERT]` families += `PADDING_FREE`. Dry-run dogfood: `scratchpad/dryrun-ENUM-TYPE.txt`. Landed: fence += the 4 axes; NOT-YET list trimmed; v2-additions bullets; refinements A/B in § Two tiers. **⇒ the format GRAMMAR is now complete** (node model D-339 + member model D-340 + validated D-341 + all D-309 axes/refinements D-342). **Remaining before the lock:** (1) the complete per-type worked-example set — struct/function/registry/file/enum/type/macro/test/assert — the operator's rollout-reference request (V1 is what rolls across the whole codebase); (2) the `[SCHEMA]` version lock — bump to `v2`, OR a clean `[SCHEMA]_[v1.0]` release since nothing is converted yet (no v1-in-the-wild to preserve — operator's call at the lock). **D-350 — P4 increment 2a: the LAYOUT fact-producer consolidated into the core (`foxtag layout`), PARITY-PROVEN straddler-exact against the Lua oracle (204 records) — and the parity gate caught a real stream-interleave corruption class — 2026-07-15.** Line-for-line C++ port of the proven Lua path (`sizeprobe.flags_for` compile-DB lookup → the TECH_DEBT-231 clang-portable LAYOUT-flag subset → `clang++ -Xclang -fdump-record-layouts` on the ABSOLUTE TU from the entry's compile dir → `recordlayout.parse` block/field/size/align semantics → `field_size` conservative resolver (PRIM widths · array-dim products · trailing-pointer=8 · census-name lookup; unresolved = partial, never guessed) → the ≤64B cache-line straddler rule). Same JSON shape as `emit_record_layout.lua` → **parity gate section 4** (skip-advisory without nvim/clang, mirroring the cache-gate's dep policy) normalizes both and diffs: **PASS, 204 records straddler-exact** — the SAME census D-320 first proved. Demo truth: `Position<64>`=128B (its LAYOUT_LOCK assert value), `ExecutionCore<64>`=68352B (TD-232's 68KB), `Order<64>`=272B with real straddlers. **The gate EARNED its keep immediately (the catch):** first run differed on exactly ONE record — `tt::OmsDrainBuckets` 147480 (lua) vs 14748 (cxx) — root cause: my `2>&1` merged clang's stdout+stderr AT THE PIPE, and a stderr diagnostic burst interleaved MID-LINE with the dump, splicing `In file included from…` into the middle of the sizeof digits (`[sizeof=14748In file…`); `vim.system` never hits it because it captures the streams SEPARATELY and concatenates after. Fix = `run_capture_split` (stderr → temp file, appended post-hoc), commented as the class it is: **never pipe-merge streams feeding a machine parser of compiler output.** (Entry-selection divergence was investigated and CLEARED — main.cpp IS db[0], absolute paths, identical flags.) Also eliminated by this producer once consumers cut over: the cache-gate's headless-nvim dependency (`emit_record_layout.lua` becomes swappable for `foxtag layout` behind the parity gate — a next-increment cutover, not this one). **Increment 2b (NEXT):** the CODEGEN producer — g++ probe-TU with the RC-A instantiation anchor / RC-C SIMD width-class / RC-E never-green-on-empty-parse — then `foxtag update` (the D-327 STRUCT layout writer) + the RC-B compile-DB regen story. Sisters: D-349 (increment 1 + the migration contract), D-320/D-321 (the layout gate + clang-layout/g++-codegen split this ports), D-336 (RC map), TECH_DEBT-231 (the flag-subset it inherits).

UNRESOLVED by black-box test: live-in-engine (correct despite concrete-`::N` failing) vs routed-through-`fp2_*`/`Money_*` (dead-in-engine); build-routing trace settles it — engine-green is NOT a deadness proof since instantiation doesn't hard-error). **NOT campaign-caused** (`git diff HEAD` on the file = empty; last touched by the Ship-A/B commits) and **NOT a new bug** — it is the **D-163 deferred `FPN_*` op-family reshape**, never executed: Ship B named the money ops `Money_*` (D-176) and left the binary `FPN_*` family untouched. The live `.v` surfaces are clean + correct: `Money_*` on `FixedPoint<10,8>` + the certified `umul_128x128_256`/`udiv256_qr`/`divmul_pow10` kernels (D-176/D-142) and the native `fp2_*` free functions on `FixedPoint<2,64>` (D-142, slice 423/0). Operator was right ("odd… we worked this out prior") — the decision log (D-142/D-163/D-176) IS the prior working-out; the miss was **procedural**: cold-read + escalated as a mystery BEFORE matching it to the log whose D-numbers (D-143/D-163/D-125) sit in the file's own header. **DISPOSITION:** (1) **TECH_DEBT-242** logged — the op-body reshape/dedup (`fp2_*` ⟷ `FPN_*` duplication + the shed-representation references), DISTINCT from TECH_DEBT-142 (that entry = the `.E.1` doc-rename tooling); (2) FixedPointN docs-pass = convert the **SETTLED parts only** (types, traits, the `Money_*` decimal path, the certified kernels, the `fp2_*` binary ops) + leave the vestigial `FPN_*` `.w` block **UNCONVERTED** with a pending-reshape marker in the `[FILE]` header (documenting to-be-reshaped code = do-it-twice); (3) the header's "ported to `.v`" line **accuracy-nudged** (the fn-family bodies were NOT ported — only the ~2,144 consumer sites rode the flip). <!-- D/C/F: D-363 -->
**D-363 — SCOPE INCREASE: the C4 DERIVED-emitter-coverage extension is pulled FORWARD from homed-advisory-backlog (D-327) into ACTIVE E.1.2.A scope — 2026-07-17 (operator: "scope increase is fine, just make sure this is captured and everything relevant is logged so we can plan and work off this").** The empty-`[DERIVED]` case (a `[STRUCT]` block with the header present but no `[SIZE]`/`[ALIGN]`/… values) is WIDESPREAD; per D-327 it was homed as a **C4-ADVISORY** backlog item (task #7). Pulled into active scope now — DERIVED completeness is the SECOND coverage axis (every layout fact materialized — the north-star's "critical we get all this right"), closed alongside the C1/C2 structural cleanup rather than deferred.
**Diagnosis (why `--fix` refreshes 0 on the leaf structs):** `check_cache_layout.py --fix` is bounded by its probe TU (`--tu main.cpp`) — a struct's `[DERIVED]` is filled ONLY if that struct is laid out in main.cpp's `-fdump-record-layouts` output. The small ML/leaf structs (`RollingTurnover`/`TradingCosts`/`RewardRecord`/…) aren't materialized in main.cpp's compilation; templates (`WelfordTracker<F>`/`RORRegressor<F>`) need a concrete instantiation to be sizeof-able (the D-318 `[INSTANTIATION]` axis). Toolchain is present (nvim/clang/g++) — a COVERAGE gap, NOT a broken arg.
**Approach (the work-off plan):** (1) a dedicated all-headers layout-probe TU (`#include` every engine header + instantiate the templates at their real `F`); (2) a `--probe-tu` mode on `check_cache_layout.py` pointed at it; (3) `--fix` then populates `[DERIVED]` corpus-wide; (4) **gate the blast radius**; (5) templates that still can't be probed → `[INSTANTIATION]` tags (D-318) or a documented C4-remainder; (6) lossless (comments-only) + validator + build-green + commit.
**Blast radius + gating (the "assuming it doesn't break other code" guard):** the fill is CORPUS-WIDE (every empty-`[DERIVED]` struct, not just this batch's 5) → a large reviewable diff, AND the cache-layout HARD gate then checks each filled struct for cross-thread straddle (H6) / `[SIZE]` drift. So the pass MUST gate its own blast radius: re-run the cache-layout gate after the fill; a NEW straddle on a cross-thread struct = a GENUINE H6 finding to triage (never auto-suppress), a benign one = fine. Comments-only + lossless still holds (only `[DERIVED]` comment lines change).
**Sequencing:** its own focused pass (task #7 promoted homed→active); the C1/C2 structural Phase-C cleanup continues in parallel/first (independent axis — the completeness gate passes with empty `[DERIVED]` = C4-advisory meanwhile).

<!-- D/C/F: D-364 -->
**D-364 — POLICY: a FUNCTION-LOCAL struct (declared inside a function's body braces) is NOT a navigable unit → EXEMPT from the conversion-completeness gate — 2026-07-18 (operator: "make a choice that works best with the proposed functionality of the plugin, that's most extensible in the future, and works with the current schema" / "whatever process makes the most sense").** The Phase-C sweep hit `StratAgg` (`NodeCtxSummaryFieldRegistry.hpp`) — a 10-field struct declared INSIDE `Summary_EmitPerStrategy`'s body. Two options surfaced: **(a)** give it a `[STRUCT]` comment-block nested in the function body, or **(b)** exempt function-local structs in `check_conversion_completeness.py`. **Chosen: (b),** on the operator's three axes: (1) **PLUGIN** — the tag-browser (`foxtag units --type STRUCT`) should list real data structures, not stack-local helpers you can't navigate to; cursor-tracking correctly resolves to the enclosing FUNCTION (a local-struct card would be an empty, reference-less card). (2) **EXTENSIBLE** — a categorical fix; every future function-local is handled automatically vs accreting awkward blocks + browser noise. (3) **SCHEMA** — the nesting model (`[CONTAINS]` on `[FILE]`/`[STRUCT]` containers; cursor→innermost-enclosing-UNIT) has no slot for a function-body-local struct; functions carry no `[CONTAINS]`. **Implemented:** `inside_function_body(lines, host_open, ln)` — the net C++ brace-count between the `[FUNCTION]` host header and the def is ≥1 ⇒ inside the fn body. A namespace-scope struct merely SITED under a mega `[FUNCTION]` block (brace-depth 0 — `RegressionFeederX` / the GCN structs / `SHA256_State`) STAYS flagged (the original Class-51 detection preserved). Ships WITH a calibration selftest (`DemoLocal6` exempt / `DemoLumped6` still flagged — the anti-Class-51 non-vacuity, `calibration-corpus-non-vacuity-discipline.md`). Blast-radius verified: cleaned 5 pre-existing false-positive RAII/staging structs (`StratAgg` + `LabelGuard`/`PhaseGuard`/`FeatureCollectCtx`/`NodeSnap`); no real unit wrongly dropped. Sister to **D-360** (the sub-registry-family + `*_LegacyV1` policy calls — same exemption-policy class). Propagated to `in-code-documentation-schema.md` § Coverage.

<!-- D/C/F: D-365 -->
**D-365 — the GRAMMAR/VOCAB 1:1-propagation discipline + the plugin as the drift watch-point — tracked scope (2026-07-18; operator: "at some point well have to update a bunch of sites when adding new vocab etc, so like we need an easy way to keep the plugin and toolchain core updated and 1:1 with the codebase... update any part of what we are currently doing... to keep inline with this process").** Adding vocab (a `[TAG]` value / a category / a `[REFERENCE]` subcat / a `[DERIVED]` axis) MUST be a ONE-site fold — one row in the schema fence or `doc-tag-vocabulary.md` — that every consumer inherits, NOT an N-site manual sync (the anti-Class-18 thesis applied to the grammar itself). **Grounded state (2026-07-18 code-read):** the Python checkers (`load_categories`/`load_vocabulary`/`load_ref_subcats`) + the `foxtag` core are fence-derived (a folded row tracks free, zero code edits); **`foxtag grammar` EMITS the fence-derived grammar as data** (the SSoT-emitter seam); `parity_check.sh` §3 already gates Python↔foxtag `grammar`. **The drift weak-link = the nvim plugin:** `tag_grammar_adapter.lua` self-describes as a MIRROR of the Python parse rule (`:1-6`), makes ZERO `foxtag` calls, and HARDCODES the unit-type set (`UNIT`, `:27`) + the `[DERIVED]`-axis rendering + drift-checks (`format_derived`/`verify`, `:63-92`). The parse RULE is grammar-agnostic (a new *category* needs no plugin edit), but a new *unit type* or *DERIVED axis* needs a manual plugin edit, and the parse-rule mirror must stay in lockstep. **Standing rule:** every consumer of the tag grammar is fence-derived / reads `foxtag grammar`+`foxtag unit`, OR earns a `parity_check.sh` section — no consumer holds grammar the fence doesn't feed it. **Homed:** the 1:1 fix (plugin → thin `foxtag grammar`/`foxtag unit` client, retiring the Lua mirror) is D-349-direction = Caramel's plugin session (D-353/D-358); the interim `parity_check.sh` plugin-grammar section + the add-vocab one-action codification = receiver tasks. Discipline home: `doc-intelligence-toolchain-architecture.md` § grammar-propagation + `tools/CLAUDE.md`. Cross-ref D-337 (one-core-N-clients), D-349 (migration contract), D-322 (the 3-surface vocab alignment).

<!-- D/C/F: D-366 -->
**D-366 — SCOPE-EXPANSION: the register-fit / access-cost per-field DERIVED axis (RC-F) — 2026-07-18 (operator: "this obviously increases scope but makes it more efficient and can cut down instruction counts by a significant margin, increasing speed" / "we'll need to rehome and place this in an appropriate place in the plan").** A NEW fact-producer + plugin axis, grounded in the operator's `Learning_cpp/projects/deep_dives` asm work (de Bruijn `×0x01010101` single-`imul` packing; the `(~A & B)==B` branchless gate — the "40-instructions → 1 `mov`" pattern). TWO linked capabilities: **(a)** a field's card shows the FIELD's own size / offset / cache-line(s) / straddle (NOT the enclosing struct's aggregate — subsumes RC-D′ / Task-#10); **(b)** an ACCESS-COST classification per field — single aligned `mov` ⟺ `size ∈ {1,2,4,8,16,32,64}` ∧ `offset mod size == 0`; a bit-packed sub-byte (`MBS_*`/`BITMAP_*` shift+mask) or misaligned field needs shift+and → the tool flags it + computes the register-aligned reconfiguration that makes access single-mov. **Scope-expansion JUSTIFIED** (operator): the instruction-count reduction → latency payoff is real + significant. **HONEST TENSION (H14 / `latency-vs-cache-decision-framework.md`):** the engine bit-packs DELIBERATELY for cache footprint / L1 residency — single-mov alignment TRADES bytes for access-ops → the axis SHOWS BOTH costs per field; the operator decides per-field, the tool NEVER blindly unpacks (not a blind-optimize; a surface-the-tradeoff). **Sequencing:** a scope-EXPANSION beyond the current C1-C4 completeness — homed as a FUTURE toolchain increment (does NOT block C4 step-2); fact-producer = receiver lane, plugin render = Caramel's session. Homed: north-star §5 RC-F + E.1.2.A plan § SCOPE-EXPANSION + Task #11. Cross-ref D-363 (C4 DERIVED-write), D-318 (`[INSTANTIATION]`), H14 (bit-packing), H6/H7 (cache/branchless), `latency-vs-cache-decision-framework.md`.
<!-- STATUS: DECIDED — scope-expansion approved + homed (north-star §5 RC-F + plan § SCOPE-EXPANSION + Task #11); execution = a future toolchain increment (after/alongside C4 step-2), NOT blocking. Fact-producer = receiver lane; plugin render = Caramel's session. No engine code. — 2026-07-18. -->

<!-- D/C/F: D-367 -->
**D-367 — PLANE becomes a FIRST-CLASS discipline-gating axis + the toolchain SELF-HOSTS in its own tag system (plane-scoped vocabulary) — 2026-07-18 (operator: "make the plane distinction first class ... they're going to have different constraints ... make the toolchain a first class citizen of this as well, considering it's almost MORE critical we get it right, to avoid errors").** The `[ENGINE]`/`[DATA_PLANE]`/`[MONITORING_PLANE]`/`[DEV_PLANE]` planes (already `[TAG]` values in `doc-tag-vocabulary.md`) are promoted to a FIRST-CLASS, discipline-GATING axis — because each plane has a distinct CONSTRAINT PROFILE (engine = full H1-H22 + latency; dev-plane/toolchain = functionality-not-latency; monitoring = engine-like determinism/coherence minus hot-path). Design (each choice mapped to a CLAUDE.md priority gradient):
- **First-class CONCEPT, path-DERIVED VALUE.** The plane is a real gating axis, but the per-unit value DERIVES from path (`CoreFrameworks/`→`[ENGINE]`, `tools/`→`[DEV_PLANE]`), overridable explicitly only where plane ≠ location — NOT hand-typed on ~200 units (that IS the N-site-manual-sync anti-pattern; auto-flow > manual sync, H15; directory structure is the plane SSoT).
- **`FOREACH_PLANE` registry (registry-tuple-as-SSoT).** Each row = `{constraint profile [which H-invariants + latency-critical? + which gates fire] + valid [TAG] vocab + [DERIVED] axis-set}` → one row auto-flows to every consumer (validator picks which checks fire; cache-gate reads "does straddle apply here"; plugin shows the right axis set). The monitoring-plane "similar-but-not-identical rules" point IS the argument for a registry over a binary flag — the profiles are graded.
- **Universal skeleton + plane-scoped vocab.** UNIVERSAL: block anatomy / parse rule / unit types (`FILE`/`STRUCT`/`FUNCTION`/…) / orient tags (`[TAG]`/`[SCHEMA]`/`[OVERVIEW]`/`[COMMENT]`/`[CODE]`/`[REFERENCE]`) / ladder. PLANE-SCOPED: the `[TAG]` VALUES (engine `HOT_PATH`/`BRANCHLESS` vs toolchain `PARSER`/`PRODUCER`/`CI_CHECK`/`GRAMMAR_DERIVED`) + the `[DERIVED]` AXES — a DIFFERENT KIND, not just different values: engine DERIVED = compiled-reality (SIZE/ALIGN/STRADDLE/BRANCHES/SIMD), toolchain DERIVED = pipeline-reality (grammar-fences-read / parity-status / planes-validated; call-graph is the one shared axis). Layered on a SHARED cross-cutting core (`FRAMEWORK_DISCIPLINE`/`SSOT`/`DETERMINISM`).
- **Structural gating** — a check proves engine-only disciplines fire ONLY on `[ENGINE]`-profile units (a `[DEV_PLANE]` struct provably never trips the straddle gate). Compile-time/structural > convention.
- **Multi-comment-syntax parser (north-star V1).** Parse rule is C++-`//`-only today; the Python checkers (`#`) + Lua plugin (`--`) need multi-prefix support to be tagged/validated. foxtag C++ is FREE (same `//`; already partially tagged).
- **Toolchain = first-class citizen (self-hosting).** The toolchain documents ITSELF in its own system — foxtag C++ now, Python/Lua after the multi-syntax parser. **RATIONALE (operator's; extends the `feedback_process_weight_by_surface_blast_radius` carve-out):** the toolchain is one-producer-N-consumers → a wrong toolchain fact fans out to EVERY consumer → almost MORE critical to get right than the engine → earns engine-grade rigor, NOT "apparatus = light."
- **Boundary (operator-raised):** the plane-first-class distinction IS the sharper engine/toolchain boundary; core-vs-tools WITHIN the toolchain (foxtag-core vs checkers/plugin) is a `[TAG]` refinement (`CORE`/`CONSUMER`), NOT a new sub-plane (don't over-split; framework-payoff-diminishing-returns).
Cross-ref D-337 (one-core-N-consumers), D-365 (grammar propagation), D-366 (RC-F), H15-H19 (registry discipline), H22/`[SCOPE]` (the sibling scale-level axis).

<!-- D/C/F: D-368 -->
**D-368 — the ISOLATED per-struct layout probe (isolation OVER the monolithic all-headers probe) + the USE-INDEPENDENCE correctness principle — 2026-07-18 (E.1.2.A C4 step-2; operator: "capture as a design spec" + "is there a way we could single out a struct... maintain a 1:1 parity with the actual compiled binary").** The C4 layout fact-producer materializes a struct's real byte layout via a PER-HEADER ISOLATED sizeof-forcing probe (`#include <cmath>/<cstdint>/<cstddef>` prelude + the header + `using namespace tt;` + `char _p[sizeof(<Name>[<64>])]`), NOT a monolithic "include every header" TU. **Why (Caramel's insight, proven):** `clang -fdump-record-layouts` only dumps a record it LAYS OUT (uses/instantiates) → the monolith covers only ~81/324 structs, is ~2min, and hits RC-B include-order fragility (`TUISnapshot`-unknown; 11% fail standalone). Isolation covers EVERY struct (sizeof-forced), in seconds, per-struct-clean. **Correctness principle:** struct layout is a PURE FUNCTION of (definition · layout-flags · template-args), USE-INDEPENDENT (no "example consumers" needed) → 1:1 with the binary by ABI determinism (D-321), PROVIDED the isolated TU matches the real build's flag/`#ifdef`-gated-field context (reuse main.cpp's flags via the compile-DB `db[1]` fallback). "Match the real code" = match the DEFINE/FLAG/ARG context, NOT the usage. `sizeof`-forcing (not a variable) sidesteps private-ctor/required-arg structs. **Homed:** the reusable technique → NEW `framework-patterns/isolated-per-struct-layout-probe.md` (first canonical = `check_cache_layout.py --isolate`, shipped `9c88436`); the edge-cases (multi-instantiation-template fill pick · per-header preludes) → Task #4. Cross-ref D-363 (C4 scope), D-321 (clang==g++ ABI-identical), D-366 (RC-F per-field facts ride this producer + the existing `function-struct-alignment-for-single-mov-access.md` discipline). *(Captured post-close on Caramel's "are you sure we captured everything" probe — the technique had landed in the tool + tasks + handoff but SKIPPED the spec she explicitly requested + the decision log; fixed same-session, not deferred — the un-logged/un-speced-decision class, AR-8 sibling.)*
<!-- STATUS: DECIDED + LANDED (--isolate shipped 9c88436, proven global/tt::/template/multi-instantiation; speced framework-patterns/isolated-per-struct-layout-probe.md; corpus-wide fill = Task #4). — 2026-07-18. -->

<!-- D/C/F: D-369 -->
**D-369 — PROVENANCE + FRESHNESS metadata: `[ORIGIN]_[AUTO|MANUAL]` + `[UPDATED]_[ISO-date]` for auto-populated content (stamp-on-change) — 2026-07-18 (E.1.2.A C4 step-3; operator, mid-fill: "we should probably make a like [UPDATED_DATE] ... for anything that autopopulates fields or auto updates the schema ... and then like a tag with [AUTO|MANUAL]").** Structuralize the provenance currently buried in the ad-hoc `(tool-refreshed — do NOT hand-edit)` PROSE into two machine-readable tags, so "who wrote this fact + when" is parseable + drift-proof (the system's own thesis: structured tags > free-text). Design (each choice grounded):
- **`[ORIGIN]_[AUTO]`** = machine-written (a fact-producer owns it; regenerable; never hand-edit) · **`[ORIGIN]_[MANUAL]`** = human-curated (a writer must NEVER clobber it). Applies wherever provenance isn't already implied by block type — the auto NON-`[DERIVED]` fields especially (`[CONSUMERS]`/`[UPSTREAM]` call-graph · `[CONTAINS]`/`[TOC]` file-structure) — and, for uniform explicit provenance + to RETIRE the drift-prone prose, on `[DERIVED]` too. Operator's "anything that autopopulates."
- **`[UPDATED]_[YYYY-MM-DD]`** = freshness stamp, **written ONLY on a real value-change** (a no-op refresh does NOT restamp). Load-bearing: it preserves the writer's PROVEN idempotency (the Class-56 non-idempotent-writer guard + the CI "run `--fix`, expect 0-diff" currency check) — a naive every-run timestamp would kill both + spew a full-corpus diff each run. ISO-8601 (sortable · locale-unambiguous · parser-safe — H9 canonical-format sibling; NOT `dd/mm/yyyy`, which also risks `/` clashing the parser). Honest tension noted: git-blame already knows per-line last-change; the in-file date earns keep only because the plugin renders it without shelling to git.
- **Naming — `[SOURCE]` REJECTED (collision):** `SOURCE` is already the `[REFERENCE]_[SOURCE]_[id]` citation subcat (§ reference-subcats) → a second meaning would overload the one word in a parse-unambiguous system → **`[ORIGIN]`.**
- **Grammar fold = ONE site:** two tokens into the ` ```category-set ` fence → the validator AUTO-derives (D-365 one-site fold; zero validator code edit). ADDITIVE — existing `[DERIVED]` blocks stay CI-green without the tags; the writer populates them; a FUTURE completeness flip can require them (Task-#6-style), not now.
- **Multi-writer (D-327 MERGE):** two auto-writers already exist — `check_cache_layout --fix` (struct LAYOUT) + the plugin `:FoxSymdepsDerived!` (call-graph); BOTH stamp `[ORIGIN]_[AUTO]` + `[UPDATED]`-on-change, each keeping the other's facts (the existing MERGE discipline). THIS session wires the `check_cache_layout` (layout) half + its selftest; the plugin half = Caramel's lane (Tasks #7/#8/#10).
- **Falls out for free:** the stale `(tool-refreshed — layout emitter cannot probe this block yet, D-327)` annotation contradiction (~81 D-327-annotated `[DERIVED]` lines; the ones `--isolate` now fills) is retired by the writer emitting `[ORIGIN]_[AUTO]` + dropping the prose.
Homed: schema § category-set (fence + table) + § WRITTEN-vs-LIVE (the stamp rule) + function-template DERIVED skeleton; `doc-intelligence-toolchain-architecture.md` § provenance-and-freshness; plan § SCOPE-IN; the writer `refresh_derived` + `--selftest`. Cross-ref D-327 (WRITTEN/LIVE + MERGE), D-363 (C4 fill), D-365 (one-site vocab fold), D-368 (--isolate producer), Class 56 (idempotency), H9 (canonical format).
<!-- STATUS: DECIDED — grammar folded (fence + table + rule) this session; `check_cache_layout` writer half (ORIGIN=AUTO + UPDATED-stamp-on-change + prose retirement) + selftest landing this session; plugin call-graph half = Caramel's lane. — 2026-07-18. -->

<!-- D/C/F: D-370 -->
**D-370 — Template-layout matching: multi-instantiation REFUSAL now + per-instantiation emit as the target — 2026-07-18 (E.1.2.A C4 dogfood; the corpus fill surfaced a wrong fact).** `--isolate --fix` exposed two previously-missed template-matching bugs in `check_cache_layout.match_layout`:
- **Multi-instantiation wrong-fact:** `RollingStats` is laid out at 4 widths (`<64,{128,256,512,1024}>` = 8640/16832/33216/65984B; the block already declares `[INSTANTIATION]_[[128][256][512][1024]]`). `match_layout` returned the FIRST record by base name → wrote an ARBITRARY single `[SIZE]` (65984B). One value is definitionally wrong for a struct used at N widths.
- **Explicit-instantiation miss:** a per-instantiation block name (`FixedPoint<2,64>`) never matched its record because clang emits `FixedPoint<2, 64>` (a space) → coverage-skipped.
**DECIDED (interim, LANDED):** `match_layout` is now two-stage — (1) EXACT space-normalized match (fills `FixedPoint<2,64>` correctly), (2) bare base-name match that returns NONE on ≥2 records → the tool REFUSES to guess: the block keeps `[INSTANTIATION]` + empty layout = a tracked C4-remainder, NEVER an arbitrary wrong fact (sister to the coverage-bounded skip for an un-probed struct). New `--selftest` case; only `RollingStats` affected corpus-wide.
**TARGET (Task #15):** per-instantiation DERIVED emit — an instantiation-aware probe that materializes each declared tuple + emits per-instantiation facts (keyed where they vary — SIZE/CACHE_LINES; collapsed where uniform — ALIGN/STRADDLE), driven by `[INSTANTIATION]`. Open sub-question: `[INSTANTIATION]` under-specifies a multi-PARAM template (RollingStats lists W, not F=64) → the tag needs the FULL sizeof-able tuple (a schema clarification). Feeds the plugin per-width render. Cross-ref D-318 (`[INSTANTIATION]`), D-321 (ABI-determinism), D-363 (C4), D-368 (--isolate).
<!-- STATUS: DECIDED — interim (multi-instantiation refusal + explicit-instantiation space-normalize) LANDED + selftested this session; per-instantiation emit = Task #15 (needs the [INSTANTIATION] full-tuple clarification). — 2026-07-18. -->

**D-371 — Toolchain cohesion sweep: `[SCHEMA]` version guard + foxtag CLASS-resolver parity fix — 2026-07-18 (E.1.2.A dogfood; operator: "make it cohesive across the entire codebase").** Running the parity/CI tools (vs hand-hunting) on the now-cohesive toolchain surfaced + closed three previously-missed drifts:
- **`[SCHEMA]_[v1]` vs `[SCHEMA]_[v1.0]` drift:** corpus was uniformly `v1.0` (D-346 LOCK) but 5 fixture files (golden + 3 tool selftests + plugin TAG-INTEGRATION) carried `v1` — the validator only PREFIX-tests `[SCHEMA]_[`, never the value. Standardized 4 files → `v1.0` (TAG-INTEGRATION left = its `v1` is one symptom of its tracked pre-fork-grammar staleness, operator lane).
- **NEW guard (`check_schema_version.py`, wired HARD into `check_session_docs`):** every converted `[SCHEMA]_[ver]` MUST == the LOCKED version, the value DERIVED from the spec's `Status: LOCKED — [SCHEMA]_[v1.0]` line (SSoT — auto-follows a bump), never hardcoded. Scans `.hpp`/`.cpp`/`.py`; `[SCHEMA]_[exempt]` opts out; `RegimeDetector.hpp` WIP-exempt. **DELIBERATELY separate from the parity-gated validator** (adding to `validate_file` would make Python flag versions foxtag doesn't → re-break parity); version-pin is a corpus-COHESION property, not a per-block grammar rule. D-137 wrapper + `--selftest` + TOOLS.md enrollment.
- **foxtag CLASS-resolver parity fix:** `parity_check.sh` caught foxtag false-flagging `[CLASS]_[23]` dangling — its resolver scanned only `RECURRING_BUG_PATTERNS.md` and missed the ~56 per-class subfiles (`recurring-bug-patterns/class-NN-*.md`, file-size-split Stage-3) that Python globs. Mirrored Python's subfile union into `foxtag.hpp` → rebuilt → **`PARITY: PASS`** (foxtag ↔ Python fully agree), a Task-#7 cutover prerequisite. (foxtag-internal dev-plane parity — captured here; not an engine/capital PARITY_ISSUES ledger item.)
Grammar-propagation reassurance: D-369's `ORIGIN`/`UPDATED` auto-propagated to BOTH validators (both derive the fence at runtime; 78 cats each; parity holds) — the anti-Class-18 design working. Cross-ref D-346, D-349/D-352 (foxtag cutover), D-365 (grammar propagation), Class 51 (non-vacuity).

<!-- D: D-372 -->
**D-372 — V1 SCOPE REDEFINITION: the toolchain becomes PERVASIVELY-CONSUMED on every surface from ONE core, and the plugin becomes a NON-CLI application of the whole toolchain — 2026-07-19 (operator brainstorm: "we have all this great tooling and documentation, but it feels like none of it matters ... can we address this ... ideally the plugin should function as a non-cli application of the toolchain").** The felt problem is NOT that the tools are unused (the data: ~68/94 are actively invoked, 0 orphaned, guarded by `check_tools_inventory` + `/tools-audit` D-115) — it is that CONSUMPTION is uneven: strong in CI, weak in the plugin (V1 pending). V1 is redefined from "the doc-tag toolchain (schema + producers + render + conversion)" to a toolchain where every capability is LIVE on the surface that fits it:
- **ONE core, no reinvention.** `tools/foxtag/` (the C++ producer, D-337) + `check_doc_metadata` (the ENGINE/grammar SSoT) ARE the core; every consumer (CLI tool · plugin · CI) PULLS from it, none rolls its own. The **2026-07-19 `check_meta_registry.py` straggler** (rolled its own `Path(__file__)` root instead of importing `ENGINE` → false `exit 2` from the workspace path) is the anti-pattern this closes — a microcosm of the whole concern. ENFORCE via a lint (a `check_*` that derives its own repo-root instead of importing the canonical `ENGINE` = red) [PROPOSED]. A `tools/core/` reorg housing {foxtag, check_doc_metadata, shared client libs} is OPTIONAL — the SUBSTANCE is the D-349 migration (every consumer reads the core) + the discipline; the rename is a signal weighed against symlink/import churn [OPEN].
- **CLI surface** — every producer/query invocable standalone (`foxtag <cmd>` + the `check_*` scripts). Largely DONE.
- **Local CI surface (NO GitHub dependency)** — the gates run on the operator's machine: pre-commit (`.githooks`, Check P) + session-close (`check_session_docs`) + a NEW **pre-push** heavier gate (full suite + sanitizers). Keeps the in-code `[SCHEMA]` docs SYNCED-WITH-CODE automatically (DERIVED auto-refresh via `check_cache_layout --fix` + completeness/version gating) — the operator's "a CI script that doesn't require pushes to github." Mostly DONE at commit/close; pre-push is the clean ADD.
- **Plugin surface = a NON-CLI APPLICATION of the whole toolchain [SCOPE INCREASE].** Not merely a render/navigate layer — the EDITOR-NATIVE front-end to everything the CLI does: browse units · query facts · refresh DERIVED · navigate refs · surface gate results, keyboard-native. This IS the "custom-IDE" endgame (north-star §8.5 / D-330), PROMOTED to the primary plugin goal. It is what makes the toolchain *felt* ("matters") — used IN the editor, not only via CLI/CI.
- **SEQUENCING: plugin-first.** Land plugin V1 CONSUMING the EXISTING producers before building more north-star producers (#12–14) — so it pays off + feels like it matters FAST (the operator's own `feedback_framework_layer_payoff_diminishing_returns`; building integration ahead of any plugin consumption is what created this feeling). This RE-PRIORITIZES #7/#8/#10 (plugin lane) AHEAD of #12–14.
- **PROPOSED refinement (operator UNSURE — left OPEN, not locked):** the two tool kinds want different surfaces — PRODUCERS (foxtag `layout`/`units`/`fields`, register-fit) → plugin + CLI; GATES (schema-version, meta-registry, completeness) → CI/commit/push, NOT the plugin (rendering a blocker in the editor is noise). Resolve at next-session design.
- **OPEN (next-session design):** the producers-vs-gates split (above) · the `tools/core/` rename · the exact pre-push gate scope · the plugin non-CLI-app feature set · the import-from-core lint.
Cross-ref: D-337 (one-producer-N-consumers) · D-349 (migration contract) · D-330 (custom-IDE endgame) · D-367 (toolchain self-hosts) · north-star §8/§8.5 · `feedback_framework_layer_payoff_diminishing_returns` · `feedback_audit_canonical_sister_before_new_infra` (the meta-registry lesson) · `project_engine_done_edge_is_the_frontier` (the counterweight — weigh this vs returning to alpha).

<!-- D: D-373 -->
**D-373 — Toolchain semantic versioning: `X.Y.Z`, MAJOR tied to the `[SCHEMA]` contract, `0.x`-until-cohesive-V1 → `1.0.0`, one version SSoT — 2026-07-19.** The toolchain (foxtag + checkers + plugin) is a dev-plane PRODUCT with N consumers; it needs a coherent version. Decision: standard 3-part semver `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`. **MAJOR is tied to the `[SCHEMA]_[vN]` contract** — a grammar bump is the one change that ripples to every consumer (plugin + CI + CLI re-coordinate) = the definition of a breaking major, so schema `v1.0 ↔ toolchain 1.x`, schema `v2 ↔ 2.x`; additive vocab rows are backward-compatible → MINOR (they don't bump the schema). MINOR = new surface/producer/capability; PATCH = bug fix. We are `0.x` until the cohesive V1 lands → `1.0.0` (the same 0-until-milestone the engine's `RELEASE_VERSION 0.3` uses). One version SSoT (`tools/TOOLCHAIN_VERSION`, a dev-plane Version.hpp analog) that every surface reads/reports (the one-core thesis applied to the version itself). The `-suffix` = the semver pre-release channel (`1.0.0-rc1`), not a required 4th component. **Rejected:** (b) the engine's deep `.F.4d.1.E…` cadence — that exists only because `ENGINE_VERSION` is wire-bound + must track every determinism phase; the toolchain has no such constraint; (c) independent per-component versions — drifts, violates one-core cohesion. Codified: `DESIGN_SPECS/doc-disciplines/toolchain-semantic-versioning.md`. Cross-ref: D-337/D-372; engine `Version.hpp` (the sister).
<!-- STATUS: DECIDED + LANDED (operator 2026-07-19) — homed in E.1.2.B + the versioning DESIGN_SPEC + tools/CLAUDE.md. **UPDATED 2026-07-19 (0.1.5 close):** `tools/TOOLCHAIN_VERSION` EXISTS and reads `0.1.0` (`0058be3`); foxtag's envelope sources `producer.version` from it, with an RC-E loud-fail (exit 2) if absent — verified. -->

<!-- D: D-374 -->
**D-374 — A codified one-action toolchain UPDATE skill (write-side orchestrator), gates stay verify-only — 2026-07-19 (operator: "we also need a codified update skill... to ensure it all gets updated at once").** The "one core, N consumers" thesis applied to the UPDATE operation: a change (new vocab row, reconverted file, new producer) must propagate to several surfaces (derived facts · indexes · grammar · parity), and a REMEMBERED N-step ritual is what drifts (the found drift: the plugin's own docs vs its code; `ExecutionCore` provenance never backfilled). Decision: ONE codified skill that regenerates all WRITTEN derived state + indexes from ground truth in dependency order (vocab → grammar → `check_cache_layout --fix` → the call-graph writer → `rebuild_doc_indexes` → `parity_check`), IDEMPOTENTLY (0-diff on re-run — the D-369 stamp-on-change rule / Class-56 guard), ending with the verify sweep (`check_session_docs.sh`) to prove a consistent state. **Guardrail:** it is an EXPLICITLY-INVOKED action, and the CI GATES stay VERIFY-ONLY (red on drift; the skill is how you FIX drift) — never a commit-hook that silently rewrites files (the same "CI flags, human acts, never auto" rule the schema uses for `[OUTDATED_INFO]`). It is the WRITE-side SISTER of the verify sweep, and a dependency of the gates (they verify its output is clean), so it lands early (`0.2`, right after the `0.1` foundation). **Rejected:** (b) each tool updated independently — the drift itself; (c) auto-rewriting commit-hook — silent file rewrites under the operator. Codified: `DESIGN_SPECS/framework-patterns/one-action-toolchain-update-orchestrator.md`. Cross-ref: D-369 (provenance/idempotency) · `check_session_docs.sh` (the verify sister) · D-337/D-372.

<!-- D: D-376 -->
**D-376 — The standardized tool-I/O schema: a versioned ENVELOPE + a uniform record-set PAYLOAD, one read/write mechanism for every tool — 2026-07-19 (operator: "even the payload needs to be a standardized IO format... general enough that all the tools can communicate and use it").** The `foxtag grammar --json` keystone generalizes: rather than each foxtag command / `check_*` producer emitting a bespoke JSON shape (→ N readers, no composition), EVERY tool speaks ONE format so a single read/write mechanism serves all — the substrate for multi-step tool composition, native CLI/plugin/AI consumption, and cross-tool communication. Two layers:
- **The ENVELOPE (outer, uniform):** `{envelope_version, kind, schema_version, producer{tool,version,command,args}, status{ok,code,findings[]}, target{paths,git_head}, payload}`. `status.findings` UNIFIES producers + gates (a gate = `kind:"verdict"` with findings in `status` + empty payload) → the plugin's gate-verdicts-as-`vim.diagnostic` (0.4) + the AI acting on structured findings both read the SAME shape. `schema_version` = the D-373 compat gate (a chain refuses an incompatible payload); `target.git_head` = staleness detection; `producer` = provenance for chaining/caching. `envelope_version` = the format's OWN version (add fields later without breaking v1 readers).
- **The PAYLOAD (inner, uniform — the deeper standardization the operator called for):** one or more NAMED RECORD-SETS — each declares a field schema (name + type, drawn from the tag vocab where it applies) + rows; a value may nest another record-set. `grammar`→{categories,unit_types,derived_axes,…} tables; `units`→a units table; `layout`→a fields table; `verdict`→a findings table. One writer emits {schema,rows}; one reader walks {schema,rows} — for ANY tool. This IS the tag system's MACHINE serialization: the vocab doc is the field dictionary; the in-code `//[CAT]_[val]` tags and these JSON record-sets are two serializations of the ONE vocabulary — adopting the I/O format EXTENDS the tag system to tool-I/O ("the code side of the vocab doc, alongside the tag system"). 
- **First canonical:** foxtag `grammar --json`. Others (units/layout/fields/codegen; the `check_*` producers) adopt behind `parity_check` (D-349). The exact payload typing/nesting is DRAFT — nailed at the 0.1.5 dive by ENUMERATING all planned payloads first (`feedback_dont_generalize_substrate_before_input_space_known`), not guessed upfront.
- **Consumed via** `foxtag_client.py` function-call helpers (Python; pybind11-ready) + a thin plugin client (Lua) + the AI reading it natively (D-375 AI-plane). Adds increment **0.1.5** to E.1.2.B.
Codified: `DESIGN_SPECS/framework-patterns/standardized-tool-io-envelope-and-payload.md` (stage-2 draft). Cross-ref: D-337/D-349 (one-core/migration) · D-373 (`schema_version`) · D-372/D-375 (V1/AI-plane) · TECH_DEBT-176 (tool-composition/unified-runner) · `in-code-documentation-schema` (the tag-serialization sibling).

Typing locked by D-380; shape amended by D-384. -->

<!-- D: D-377 -->
**D-377 — `.toolbus/` — the cross-tool rendezvous dir (gitignored, latest-wins) — 2026-07-19 (operator: "an output and cross communication folder would be nice").** A gitignored directory where a tool drops its latest ENVELOPE (D-376) as `<kind>[--<scope>].json` (`grammar.json`, `layout--ExecutionCore.json`), so consumers read tool outputs from ONE canonical place instead of re-invoking. Kept a CACHE, not an IPC system: **latest-wins · gitignored · regenerable** (delete anytime → tools rewrite; no history, no GC — bounded by #kinds); **staleness is free** — each envelope carries `target.git_head`, so a reader knows if a cached fact is current or stale. Readers: the plugin (vocab from `grammar.json`, no subprocess per keystroke) · the AI (one canonical read location — D-375) · multi-step chains (step 2 reads step 1's file). The **dev-plane twin of the decoupling endgame's published-state dir** (`plans/_future/2026-05-12-decoupling-endgoal-roadmap.md` — per-run tailable artifacts; engine publishes, viewers read) — same pattern, toolchain scale. Run-scoping / history DEFERRED until a concrete consumer needs it (the decoupling endgame will — add it THEN, `feedback_dont_generalize_substrate_before_input_space_known`). Part of E.1.2.B 0.1.5. Cross-ref: D-376 (the envelope it stores) · D-372/D-375 · `headless-engine-viewer-split-pattern` (the engine sibling).

The decision itself STANDS. -->

<!-- D: D-378 -->
**D-378 — Payload kinds catalog (graph kinds included), `kind`-as-vocabulary, shared emit/read helpers, integrity = content-hash-NOT-HMAC — 2026-07-19 (operator: "shouldn't there be a DAG / code graph / call map … in the payload? … hmac or sha256 … or is this needlessly complex?").** Refinements to D-376: **(1) graph kinds** — a call-graph / DAG / trace / reference-graph / blast-radius is a payload whose record-set is EDGES (`{from,to,edge_kind}`); no special graph infra (a graph = rows of edges). They exist scattered (plugin clangd call-hierarchy · `CODE_MAP` · `gen_code_map` · foxtag `RefIndex`) but aren't unified foxtag kinds yet — the `0.1.5` enumeration catalogs them. Starter kinds: `grammar·units·layout·fields·codegen·verdict·callgraph·depgraph·refgraph·blast_radius·instantiation`. **(2) `kind` is a registered VOCABULARY** (SSoT, 1-row-add). **(3) one write + one read path** — a shared envelope-emit helper (write-side SSoT, `foxroots`-sibling) + the uniform reader; not N hand-rolled emitters. **(4) Integrity = CACHING not crypto:** an OPTIONAL `content_hash` (SHA-256) for dedup/staleness — but NOT HMAC. HMAC authenticates capital-bearing WIRE data (the engine's H9 stamps/snapshots — a real adversary + cross-binary determinism); the `.toolbus/` is local/gitignored/regenerable/no-wire/no-capital → nothing to authenticate, so HMAC is needless-complexity (wrong threat model). Content-hash yes; HMAC + bespoke binary parser no (payload is JSON; the record-set reader IS the parser). Homed in the spec. Cross-ref: D-376/D-377 · H9 (the engine wire-HMAC — the contrast) · `CODE_MAP`/`gen_code_map`/`RefIndex` (the scattered graph producers to unify).

Still unbuilt: the GRAPH kinds (`callgraph`/`depgraph`/`refgraph`/`blast_radius`) + the optional `content_hash` — no consumer until 0.4+. -->

<!-- D: D-379 -->
**D-379 — First-class toolchain artifacts get PER-ITEM schema tracking + independent versioning — 2026-07-19 (operator: "treat these as design patterns we wanna track, for schema tracking and versioning per individual item, since they're becoming first class alongside engine code … removes decision fatigue + holding everything in my head, since i am a solo engineer").** The toolchain's design elements (the envelope format · each payload `kind`'s record-set schema · the tag grammar · the `.toolbus/` layout · each producer's output contract) are now FIRST-CLASS alongside engine code, so they earn engine-grade tracking: each is a VERSIONED DESIGN_SPEC (pattern-codification lifecycle) AND carries its OWN schema version, evolving INDEPENDENTLY — not one monolithic version. Concretely THREE independent envelope version axes, each tracking ONE concern: **`envelope_version`** (the envelope format) · **`schema_version`** (the `[SCHEMA]` tag-grammar the payload's vocab speaks — D-373) · **NEW: a per-`kind` payload-schema version** (each record-set kind — units/layout/verdict/callgraph — evolves + is checked independently, carried in the record-set's self-describing schema). Plus `TOOLCHAIN_VERSION` (the product — D-373) + each DESIGN_SPEC's own `version:`. A consumer/multi-step chain checks exactly the axis it depends on; a kind evolves without perturbing others. **WHY (operator's own rationale, captured verbatim in intent):** good software doesn't REQUIRE this apparatus, but per-item tracking+versioning is directly proportional to how *accessible* the toolchain is, and it REMOVES decision-fatigue + externalizes the "what state is each piece in" a solo engineer would otherwise hold in their head — externalized cognition + correctness risk-control (`user_structure_is_correctness_risk_control_for_capital`). Makes D-373's versioning PER-ITEM. Homed in the versioning + tool-I/O specs. Cross-ref: D-373 (versioning) · D-376/D-378 (envelope/payload/kinds) · `pattern-codification-lifecycle.md` (the spec-versioning discipline) · `toolchain-semantic-versioning.md`.

<!-- D: D-380 -->
**D-380 — The `0.1.5` tool-I/O typing LOCK: single-doc JSON · uniform record-set TABLE · schema-as-DATA registry · comments-only-never-code — 2026-07-19 (operator informing pass, D1–D4).** Locks the substrate shape after the enumerate-first grounding (units NDJSON `{file,type,name,open_line,close_line,tags}` · grammar scalar-counts→SETS · layout/fields clang MAPS · codegen VOLATILE · verdict text-only-today · foxtag `RefIndex` the near-ready graph). **(1) Serialization = single-doc plain JSON** [D1] — NOT NDJSON (a stream style for huge logs; `.toolbus/` is a small latest-wins file read whole → one wrapped doc carries the envelope metadata NDJSON has no home for) and NOT YAML (needs a C++/Lua dep the JSON path doesn't; non-deterministic to emit byte-identically → breaks the content-hash/staleness discipline; YAML's human-authoring win doesn't apply to a machine-ONLY bus — the human-authored surfaces [frontmatter · the `[TAG]` grammar] stay their own formats). **(2) Payload = uniform record-set TABLE** [D2] — `{schema, rows}` where the schema IS the fixed field-layout (operator's analogy, correct: a bit-packed instruction where each slot has a defined purpose — the `SHIFT_*`/`MASK_*` discipline, for records) and rows are conforming instances; name-as-a-COLUMN not a map-key; sorted-deterministic. A record-set = an X-macro registry (`FOREACH_CFG_FIELD`) serialized. layout/fields' map→rows reshape happens at each producer's GATED cutover (D-349), leaving their parity-proven shapes untouched until then. **(3) Schema-as-DATA registry** [D4, operator's design] — the emitter READS each kind's schema+version from a registry and emits accordingly; NEVER hardcodes a schema into emitter code. Extends the toolchain's founding "grammar-DERIVED, never hardcoded" law (foxtag reads categories from the schema fences at runtime) UP one level to the payload + CI-gate schemas; add/evolve a kind = a registry row, every producer+consumer tracks it (the `FOREACH_CFG_FIELD` auto-flow, for payloads). Start minimal (grammar + the verdict/findings schema so the gates have a target), grow per kind. This is the MECHANISM for D-379's per-kind self-describing versioning, and GENERALIZES beyond tool-I/O — the CI gates + the tag/vocab rules are the same definition-as-data discipline (operator: "can be applied elsewhere too, like the tag system for the CI gates"). **(4) Comments-only-never-code, elevated to a named rule** [D3] — tooling WRITES only comments / derived-fact blocks / index docs (lossless-gated), NEVER generates or rewrites engine LOGIC. `codegen` (instr/branch/SIMD probe) is read-only + LIVE-only (never persisted — flips with `-O`/`-march`); `check_cache_layout --fix` rewrites the derived COMMENT, never the struct. Auto-generated engine code is a Knight-adjacent trust hole on a capital path (same verification burden as hand-written → buys nothing) — operator skepticism is calibrated correctly; make it structural. Homed: `standardized-tool-io-envelope-and-payload.md` (payload/registry) + `toolchain-semantic-versioning.md` (axes) + `tools/CLAUDE.md` (comments-only law already present — add the schema-registry). Cross-ref: D-376/D-378/D-379 · D-373 · the grammar-DERIVED law.

**D-381 — V1 = "felt in the editor"; the heavy substrate floats to `1.1` (the scope seam) — 2026-07-19 (operator: "scope is going up a lot, but this is getting to the point i think i want for a v1").** As the vision filled in (tag-system-AS-graph · auto-visualizations in nvim · per-field cacheline/register-fit breakdowns), the operator's own signal marks the `1.0.0` line: **V1 is "felt in the editor" — the plugin live on ONE core + tag-native surfaces + the register-fit/cacheline cards (~through `0.5`).** The deeper substrate (`0.8` self-hosting · `FOREACH_PLANE` · the multi-comment-syntax parser · exotic viz) FLOATS to `1.1+` — the natural seam the plan already flagged. **Sequencing, NOT cutting** (`feedback_no_defer_for_effort`): the whole vision stays on the roadmap; the seam just makes `1.0.0` a real shippable milestone (the felt-editor payoff) instead of a receding horizon, keeping the framework-payoff curve honest (`feedback_framework_layer_payoff_diminishing_returns` — ship the felt V1, keep adding in `1.x`). The tag-as-graph + cacheline-per-field ideas are AFFIRMED as high-value — they serve the operator's OWN engine work directly (H6 false-sharing / register-fit made visible at edit-time) — and land at `0.4`/`0.5`; the `0.1.5` substrate is precisely what makes each a render-a-payload exercise, not a new analysis. <!-- D: D-382 -->
**D-382 — The `0.1.5` tool-I/O design, RESOLVED by a 3-I→3-A armed cascade — 2026-07-19.** An armed I→A cascade (3 investigative + 3 adversarial agents) gated the `0.1.5` sidecar; it flipped two i-class recs, caught a non-compiling line + a wrong shape propagated into the spec, and converged. Resolutions: **(1) CRUX — `foxtag grammar --json` emits a COMPLETE self-describing envelope** (not a raw payload Python wraps), with the envelope FRAME data-driven off the shared registry + ONE envelope definition. This preserves SSoT — the `Version.hpp` model: two readers (foxtag C++ + `toolio.py`) of ONE source, NOT two truths. Chosen for foxtag-as-self-describing-product (D-373) + because the plugin reads foxtag stdout DIRECTLY at attach (a real consumer). The A-1 "one emitter" purity concern is satisfied by the data-driven frame (the schema mirror — the real Class-18 — is killed by the shared registry regardless). **(2) PAYLOAD = O3** — the vocab sets (`categories/ref_subcats/concern/surface`) PLUS the node-model set (`unit_types`+`openers`) from foxtag's EXISTING free functions (`foxtag.hpp:230,234`). Kills the plugin's hardcoded-`UNIT` Lua mirror at `0.1.5` (not 0.3) + fixes a live `{ASSERT}` drift (plugin set stale by one type). `derived_axes`/`ladder` DEFER (no producer, no consumer). Refutes both the i-class O2 (omits the one set the plugin needs) and O4 (fence-encoding reverses the deliberate prose-mirror, D-349 pt 5). **(3) NO `.toolbus/` in `0.1.5`** — grammar is cheap + session-stable, so a file-cache is premature infra (gitignore/atomic-write/cross-repo-path machinery for zero benefit); the plugin reads grammar via subprocess-at-attach (its chosen transport, atomic-by-construction, dodges Landmine-5). `.toolbus/` (D-377 STANDS) lands at `0.4`/`0.5` with its first VOLATILE-fact consumer (`layout--<target>.json`), WITH atomic temp-rename + the correct WORKSPACE-repo `.gitignore`. Substrate ≠ cache; only the cache was mis-sized. **(4) `verdict` = a declared registry row + a selftest FIXTURE (positive+negative per kind), NOT a producer** — a `check_*` `--json` producer is `0.7`/`0.3` gate-cutover work; D-380's "so the gates have a TARGET" means a schema to aim at, not one already hit. Findings live in **`status.findings`** (spec §1), resolving the sidecar-§3b-vs-spec-§1 contradiction — so grammar's own envelope exercises the findings path on the happy path. **(5) Registry = plain JSON read via foxtag's EXISTING `json_parse`** (`foxtag_layout.hpp:71`, promoted core-ward) — the "mirror trap" fear was over-stated; zero new dep. Build by EXTEND (`json_escape`/`unit_json` writer + `parity-dump` pattern + `_run`), not new. Non-vacuity = content-diff vs the Python authority, never `|categories|==78` (Class-51 count-proxy). Homed: the resolved sidecar `subplans/2026-07-19-E.1.2.B-sidecar-0.1.5-tool-io-substrate.md` + spec `standardized-tool-io-envelope-and-payload.md`. **D-384 — The D-383 blast-radius re-sweep result: architecture SURVIVES + 6 amendments; the `findings/1` verdict-model restructure + `schema_version` single-source + the fact-vs-workflow C++/Python boundary — 2026-07-19.** The re-sweep (RS-I1 toolchain / RS-I2 tag-system / RS-A adversary) confirmed the D-382 architecture holds (envelope + registry + O3 + no-`.toolbus` + subprocess all survive; NET-POSITIVE on the tag system — kills 2 drifted Lua node-model copies + fixes the `{ASSERT}` drift + closes a currently-ungated node-model parity hole). Amendments landed before implementation: **(1) `findings/1` restructure [STRUCTURAL, not belt-and-suspenders].** `status.findings` is a CROSS-CUTTING envelope field (every envelope carries it), so its schema lives ONCE at the envelope level (`findings/1`), governing `status.findings` for EVERY kind — NOT as a per-kind payload table. Modeling `verdict` as a payload kind (the resolved sidecar's error) was a CATEGORY error that forced two registry container shapes (`tables` vs `table`) + a kind-branch in `read()`/`validate()` + a self-contradicting spec (`:44` "verdict→findings table" vs `:36` findings-in-status). The fix REMOVES a special-case (fewer moving parts): every PAYLOAD kind is uniformly `{tables:{…}}`; `verdict` = `kind:"verdict"` + empty payload + populated `status.findings`; add-a-kind → one row, findings comes free (the `FOREACH_CFG_FIELD` auto-flow signature). **(2) `schema_version` SINGLE-SOURCE-derived [closes a D-346 second-source risk].** The envelope's `schema_version` IS the D-346-LOCKED grammar version; a hardcoded `"v1.0"` in the C++ + Python emitters plants two mirrors of a locked string (the exact drift `check_schema_version.py` exists to kill). Derive it at emit from the ONE schema-doc SSoT line (`in-code-documentation-schema.md:17`), reusing the `check_schema_version.py::locked_version()` seam — foxtag's `load_grammar` ALREADY reads that doc (zero extra read). A `[SCHEMA]` bump = edit ONE line; both sides runtime-read it → NO recompile (data change, not code), NO N-file sweep. In the EMIT path only (not the parity-checked `validate` path, or byte-parity re-breaks). **(3) C++/Python boundary = FACT-vs-WORKFLOW [operator-articulated].** Tri-language (C++ foxtag core + Python checkers + Lua plugin) is the EXPECTED shape of a real system, not a smell. What solidifies in the C++ core = FACTS (parse rule · grammar · layout probe — single-sourced, repeated, foundational); what stays a Python wrapper = WORKFLOWS (corpus-walk · doc-rewrite · orchestration · CI-cadence, no latency budget). D-349 is the machinery that PROMOTES a fact from its Python bootstrap into foxtag once proven (behind parity). **(4-6) Mechanical:** `toolio.py` → `tools/toolio.py` (module tier — `tools/lib/` is a NON-recursive-glob guard blind spot for `check_import_from_core`+`check_tools_inventory`; `toolio_schemas.json` stays `tools/lib/`, data tier) + a TOOLS.md row + `toolio_selftest.sh` wrapper; parity diffs the node-model BY MEMBERS vs the `check_code_tag_blocks.py` authority (not counts, not vocab-only); DROP the `openers` payload table (the plugin derives closable from `unit_types.closable` — `CODE` ∈ openers ∉ unit_types = a re-drift foot-gun); `json_parse` promotion = the whole 4-symbol cluster (MOVE not copy); `cmd_grammar` gains a `roots` arg; `TOOLCHAIN_VERSION` loud-fails if missing (no silent-empty). Spec `:44`/`:46` corrected (the fan-out surface). NOT a re-cascade (contained internal-consistency + placement/completeness fixes, all sweep-specified). Homed: resolved sidecar + spec. Cross-ref: D-382 (the design it amends) · D-383 (the cadence that found it) · D-346/D-349 · RS-I1/RS-I2/RS-A.

<!-- D: D-385 -->
**D-385 — META (M10): delegate implementation where the acceptance oracle is TOTAL; hand-review where it is PARTIAL — 2026-07-19 (operator: *"the agents you dispatched dont have the context from this conversation that is needed for correct work"* · *"normally it would be fine if 'feature works' is the passing metric, but this is correctness critical first, so works has a large defined area, and we want it more correct than just a 1 or 0"*).** **The evidence (E.1.2.B 0.1.5/0.3, surfaced only because the operator pushed for verification):** 0.1.5's C++ twin was *safely* delegated because it had a **TOTAL** oracle — byte-identity against the Python reference either matches or it doesn't, no judgment. Where the oracle was **PARTIAL**, a context-carrying hand read found real defects that every automated signal missed. In `nodemodel.lua` — all tests green, parity green, sweep green — reading the code surfaced (a) a **subprocess-storm hang**: `parse` calls `is_unit()` per token → `model()`, and a *failed* fetch is deliberately never cached, so a **present-but-broken** foxtag re-spawns it with a **2s blocking wait per token** (harmless when foxtag is merely absent, an editor freeze when it is broken — and worse once 0.5 adds cursor-tracking); and (b) a hardcoded `FoxML_Trader_v2` repo name inside the **publishable** plugin — the same trader-coupling class the a-class had already flagged for the path anchor. Neither is expressible as a test assertion.

**A CORRECTION, recorded verbatim because it is the sharpest evidence of all.** This entry originally cited *"7 functions added to tag-CONVERTED files carry no orient block, including `cmd_grammar` whose 4 siblings do."* **That was FALSE.** An unanchored `rg '\[SCHEMA\]_\[v1'` matched **selftest fixture string literals** (`"// [SCHEMA]_[v1.0]\n// [FUNCTION]_[F]…"` inside `static const Case SELFTEST[]`), not orient blocks. Against the gate's real anchored selector (`^// \[SCHEMA\]_\[v1`), **ZERO** C++ files under `tools/` are converted — foxtag is correctly un-converted (self-hosting is later), and nothing was violated. The false finding was authored **while documenting this very discipline**: an assertion from a token COUNT instead of reading what the matches WERE (`feedback_verify_by_context_not_count`). **A partial check misleads its own auditor** — which is the thesis, demonstrated on the thesis.

**What survives, independently verified from source:** `check_conversion_completeness` checks only **structs / FOREACH registries** (C1 lumped · C2 missing-block · C3 missing-DERIVED) — **FUNCTIONS are never checked for a missing block.** That is a *latent* hole (nothing currently violates it), sibling to TECH_DEBT-245's gitignored blind spot in the same gate. **The rule:** before delegating implementation, classify the acceptance oracle — **TOTAL** (a deterministic check that fails on ANY deviation: byte-identity, golden output, parity-of-two-implementations) → delegation is safe; **PARTIAL** (tests + gates covering a subset) → a **context-carrying hand-review before commit is MANDATORY; green is NOT done.** In this codebase the checks are partial nearly everywhere, so implementation delegation ALWAYS requires the review pass. **Why an agent cannot self-close this:** the only signal that the un-blocked functions were wrong is the judgment *"this project is converting toward full coverage, so new code should land converted rather than adding to the backlog"* — that lives in the conversation and the sprint intent, not in the spec handed to the agent nor in any check. **Corollary:** the fix for a partial oracle is to MAKE it total (D-386), not to stop delegating. Cross-ref: M8 (armed scout-first) · `feedback_passing_test_is_not_verification` · `feedback_adversarial_framing_default_for_checks` · `feedback_delegate_via_locked_spec_at_implementation` (this BOUNDS it) · TECH_DEBT-245 · D-386.
**NUMBERING TOMBSTONE — this discipline was FIRST WRITTEN AS `M9`; it is `M10`.** The `M9` slot was already claimed: the `.E.0.10` close (2026-06-12) logged *"earmark M8→M9 for enumerate-set"* (this same log, line ~1293) and `DESIGN_PHILOSOPHY.md:819` has carried `feedback_enumerate_set_before_categorical_claim` (cataloged **AR-1**) as "the standing **M9-candidate**" since 2026-05-29. Both predate this entry by 5–7 weeks, so the prior claim wins and this discipline moved to **M10**. Caught 2026-07-19 by an operator-prompted completeness sweep, NOT by any gate — nothing checks citable-ID uniqueness. **This is H21's failure mode transposed to the META plane:** a citable identifier carrying two meanings, so a reader resolving `M9` gets the wrong discipline — the same class the engine forbids on wire/persistence slots. Recorded rather than silently corrected because commits `3ba0fe8`/`4aecc95` and any prose written before this fix still say `M9` and must resolve here. Structural follow-up = the citable-ID integrity guard (see the ID-namespace sweep this session).
