<!-- D: D-386 -->
**D-386 — GOLDEN OUTPUT BASELINES for the toolchain — make the oracle TOTAL — 2026-07-19 (operator: *"we need golden baselines for the tools and stuff to ensure nothing breaks on updates since they ideally need to function the same everytime unless we specify a new golden needs to be set, similar to the engine code"*).** **Grounded gap:** the toolchain today has (a) **selftests** — prove a tool CAN fail on a planted bad; (b) **parity** — proves the C++ and Python implementations AGREE; (c) **exception baselines** (`tools/lib/import_from_core_baseline.txt`, `completeness_baseline.txt`) — grandfather known-bad findings, shrinking. **NONE of these pin a tool's OUTPUT.** So a change to what a tool EMITS passes every gate as long as both implementations change together and no new violation appears — exactly the hole a delegated implementation falls through (D-385). The ENGINE already has the missing discipline (`tests/schema_golden/golden_*.hpp`; `check_determinism.sh` FP-golden native==generic cross-opt-level). **Decision: adopt OUTPUT GOLDENS for the fact-producers** — a blessed, committed expected-output for a FIXED input; any deviation REDs; **re-blessing is an EXPLICIT act** (`--bless`, which SHOWS the diff) so a golden is never rubber-stamped. **Normalize the volatile envelope frame** (`target.git_head` · `producer.version` · `status`) out of the comparison — reuse the parity §3b exclusion, else every commit REDs. **Distinct from the exception-baselines:** those answer *"which known-bad is tolerated"*; a golden answers *"is the output still what we blessed."* **First targets (highest fan-out first):** `foxtag grammar --json` (the 0.1.5 envelope — already deterministic + normalized), then `units`/`layout`/`fields`, `gen_code_map`, and the `check_*` report shapes. **Home: E.1.2.B `0.2`** (the update-orchestrator increment already owns "wire `parity_check.sh` into session-close"); land it EARLY — it is a **verification substrate**, so its value is proportional to the work that follows (0.4→0.7 all inherit it). Cross-ref: D-385 (the partial-oracle problem this closes) · `feedback_golden_master_over_reimplemented_oracle` · TECH_DEBT-245 · D-374 (the write-side orchestrator that will own the `--bless` action).
<!-- STATUS: DECIDED (operator 2026-07-19) — output goldens for the fact-producers; home = 0.2, landed early as a verification substrate. NOT built. -->

<!-- D: D-387 -->
**D-387 — Reframe `0.2` into the TOOLCHAIN CONTRACT + GATE LAYER (contract docs · output goldens · ONE wired tools-gate) — 2026-07-19 (operator: *"we need better documentation and design specs for the actual toolchain at this point, since its becoming first class, and enables the further scaling and development of the actual engine … the actual engine code is going to rest on this infrastructure so we need to get it right"*).** **The problem, evidenced:** nearly every wrong turn in the `0.1.5`/`0.3` session traced to toolchain contracts existing ONLY in source — foxtag's cwd-sensitivity (cost a debug cycle), the ANCHORED converted-file selector (produced a **false finding**), the completeness gate's struct-only coverage, its gitignored blind spot (TECH_DEBT-245), and the exception-baseline-vs-output-golden confusion. The toolchain became load-bearing infrastructure **without acquiring the contract + enforcement layer the engine already has.** **The gap:** engine = invariants doc (H1–H22) + golden fixtures (`schema_golden/`, FP-golden) + wired pre-commit/sweep; toolchain = orientation-only (`tools/CLAUDE.md`) + selftests/parity + a **MANUAL** tools-gate. **Decision — `0.2` becomes three coupled deliverables:** **(1) CONTRACT docs** — per-tool consumes / emits / guarantees / failure-modes. Seeded NOW by the harvested invariants written into `tools/CLAUDE.md` § "Tool invariants + gotchas". The machine-readable half ALREADY exists (`toolio_schemas.json` + the envelope, `0.1.5`); the prose is the missing half. **(2) OUTPUT GOLDENS** (D-386) — pin fact-producer output; `--bless` re-blessing that SHOWS the diff; volatile envelope frame normalized out. **(3) ONE WIRED tools-gate** — **EXTEND `parity_check.sh`** (selftests + parity + goldens) and wire it into commit/session-close. **Do NOT add a parallel `gate_check.sh`:** `check_session_docs.sh` gates docs/plans, `parity_check.sh` gates the tools — a third gate is the parallel-infrastructure anti-pattern (`feedback_audit_canonical_sister_before_new_infra`). The D-374 update-orchestrator STAYS in `0.2` as the WRITE side; the gate is the VERIFY side; siblings, landing together. **Why early:** it is a **verification substrate** — `0.4`–`0.7` and the resumed engine SoA work all inherit it; and per D-385 it is the structural fix that makes delegation safe (it converts partial oracles into total ones). Cross-ref: D-385 · D-386 · D-374 · TECH_DEBT-245.

<!-- D: D-388 -->
**D-388 — SCOPE CALLS: the knowledge base stays ONE repo (split PARKED); the toolchain gets NUMBERED invariants but NOT a parallel apparatus — 2026-07-19 (operator: *"its almost becoming 3 repos needed at this point, but i consider them part of the centralized knowledge base … considering this is like 5 products all independent of each other"* → *"for setting it up for scalability, the current is probably fine, but a more defined split would maybe eventually be needed but thats not the concern now"*).** **(a) REPO SPLIT — PARKED; do not re-litigate.** The five surfaces (engine · foxtag C++ core · the Python checker family · the nvim plugin · the workspace knowledge base) are **NOT independent products — they are five SURFACES of one system**, which is precisely the one-core thesis (D-337: one parser, N consumers). The `[SCHEMA]` tag grammar is a CONTRACT spanning engine↔foxtag↔checkers↔plugin; splitting into repos would force versioning that contract ACROSS repo boundaries — the exact coordination cost the architecture exists to remove. The knowledge base (decision log · DESIGN_SPECS · memories) is the connective tissue and spans all five (D-372 spans three of them; D-385 applies everywhere). **The three repos that ALREADY exist cost real money this session:** the `tools/` symlink caused the Landmine-5 root-resolution bug the 0.1.5 fork had to solve; the plugin's separate repo is exactly WHY it is un-gated by workspace CI (nothing outside it runs its tests); one logical change (`0.3`) required two commits in two repos. **The only future split trigger:** a surface gaining EXTERNAL contributors on an independent release cycle — realistically only the plugin — and even then the move is **publish-from-a-subtree**, never fragmenting the knowledge base. **(b) TOOLCHAIN APPARATUS — number the invariants, fork nothing else.** The real gap was that the toolchain's disciplines existed as UNNUMBERED prose → uncitable → unenforceable (which is how T2 grammar-DERIVED and T4 migration-contract got rediscovered the hard way in `0.3`). Fixed: the **T1–T12** index in `tools/CLAUDE.md` (the dev-plane's H1–H22). **Deliberately NOT built:** a parallel anti-pattern catalog (the global `Class N` numbering already carries the toolchain's two most-used shapes — Class-18 mirror, Class-51 vacuous guard; forking it IS the parallel-infrastructure anti-pattern) and a separate memory namespace (same dir, same convention, already documented in `tools/CLAUDE.md`). Design specs already exist (six). **One fix, three deliberate non-builds** — `feedback_structural_fix_over_belt_and_suspenders` applied to process infrastructure. Cross-ref: D-337 · D-385/386/387 · TECH_DEBT-245/246 · `feedback_audit_canonical_sister_before_new_infra`.

<!-- D: D-389 -->
**D-389 — CITABLE-ID INTEGRITY is a first-class guarded property; ONE extended tool, not a parallel checker; namespace-scope overlap handled by CONVENTION not mechanization — 2026-07-19 (operator: *"we should probably fix the registry ID right?"* → *"we probably need to figure a way to prevent similar issues in the future right?"*).**

**What forced it.** Finishing M10's codification uncovered that the *identifier system itself* is unguarded. This codebase treats citable IDs as a knowledge-base primitive — you write `H21`, `Class 28`, `M7`, `AR-1`, `D-385`, `TECH_DEBT-194` and the reader resolves it to one canonical meaning. **Nothing enforces that meaning is unique, defined, or reachable.** A corpus sweep (every finding opened at both sites, never counted) found: **`M9` defined twice** with different disciplines, committed; **`D-1`..`D-13` defined twice** across two decision logs with *genuinely diverging* content (`D-7` = opposite dispositions on the ImGui GUI) while the older log's `status:` still read `pre-plan-body`; **`WH-7`** cited as canonical by 5 live docs with no SSoT row; **8 dangling `TECH_DEBT-nnn`** citations pointing at entries that do not exist; **5 split-brain entries** living in both `open.md` and `closed.md` (`-107` says 49-globals-OPEN in one and 47-globals-CLOSED in the other); **`D-245`/`D-246`** minted-in-frontmatter then never created, leaving reusable slots. Three of the four "clean" namespaces were clean only by luck of low churn.

**Why CI never saw any of it.** `tools/check_code_tag_blocks.py:170` builds its TECH_DEBT membership set by **MENTION** — `re.findall(r"TECH_DEBT-(\d+)", _read(f))` over raw ledger text — so `TECH_DEBT-101` resolves purely because another entry's prose mentions it. Same shape at `:176` for `Class N`. A membership set that admits anything it has ever seen **cannot go red**: Class-51, and the direct cause of the blind spot. It also resolves `D-nnn` against the **union** of all decision logs, so a dual-defined ID always resolves — to an arbitrary one.

**The decisions.**
1. **Citable-ID integrity is a guarded property, not a convention.** This is **H21 transposed to the doc/meta plane** — H21 forbids reusing a persistence/wire identifier because an old reader resolves the slot to the wrong meaning; an ambiguous `D-7` or `M9` is the same failure with a human as the reader. The engine plane has a CI guard for it (`check_identifier_retirement.py`); the doc plane had none.
2. **ONE tool, EXTENDED — not a second checker.** `check_code_tag_blocks.py` already builds a membership index for 5 of 8 namespaces and already resolves dangling refs; it just never looks at prose. Lift `_build_ref_index()`/`_ref_resolves()` into `tools/lib/citable_ids.py`, harden the by-mention sets, add the 3 missing namespaces, build the prose scanner on the shared module. A parallel checker would be the Class-18 mirror at the tooling layer, and `feedback_structural_fix_over_belt_and_suspenders` forbids the redundant-second-layer shape. Merged with the §11.5 coverage checker for the same reason — they share their entire substrate. → TECH_DEBT-249, sequenced into `0.2`. **[PATH SUPERSEDED: the module lands at `tools/citable_ids.py`, NOT `tools/lib/` — see C-389. `tools/lib/` is the DATA tier and a non-recursive-glob guard blind spot.]**
3. **Namespace-scope overlap is NOT mechanized.** `M1`-`M9` mean meta-disciplines globally but MEDIUM finding IDs inside audit reports; `B12`-`B17` are pillars globally but work-item IDs in one synthesis; `T1`-`T13` collided *on arrival* (toolchain invariants vs CHANGELOG test-sections vs a work item). A checker cannot tell scopes apart, and forcing it to guess produces the false-positive noise that gets a guard ignored. **Fix = prefix finding IDs in the audit-report template (`F-M1`, never `M1`).** Convention where mechanization would lie — the deliberate counterpart to decision 1, not an exception to it.
4. **`registry_id:` becomes the parse surface.** Verified it currently has **zero consumers** — no tool, skill, or hook reads it, it is absent from `doc-frontmatter-convention.md`, and `check_doc_metadata.py` requires only `{type, stage, version, established, tags, surface}`. So it was **never breaking CI**; it is an inert convention at **2-of-192** adoption. Add it to the frontmatter convention, backfill ID-bearing specs, and let the guard key off it.

**Fixed immediately (small, in-flight, verified):** the `M9`→`M10` renumber with a tombstone · `D-1`..`D-13` disambiguated (v1 marked `superseded` + `superseded_by:` + do-not-cite banner — one frontmatter line retiring 13 ambiguous IDs) · `WH-7` enrolled · 4-of-8 defective §11.5 rows repaired · M5's missing gate trigger added. **Deferred with a home:** the 8 dangling + 5 split-brain TECH_DEBT IDs need per-ID operator triage (re-home vs tombstone; HISTORICAL-in-a-frozen-postmortem vs LIVE) — that is a judgment call and the corpus that will prove the tool's teeth.

**The meta-lesson.** The `-101` instance was found once, patched once (`closed.md:1300` is literally titled *"cited TECH_DEBT-101 exists in NO ledger file — re-homed here"*), and **never swept** — so eight siblings survived. Patching an instance without sweeping its class is the M7 signal, and this is that escalation.

Only the per-ID HISTORICAL-vs-LIVE calls stay operator-facing, at triage time. -->

<!-- D: D-390 -->
**D-390 — DOC ROT IS THREE MECHANISMS, NOT ONE; guard GOING-FORWARD via baseline-and-red-on-new, do NOT mass-repair history — 2026-07-19 (operator: *"i just wanna ensure the documentation side is streamlined so we dont end up using old data"* → *"for historical data thats no longer current i guess its not extremely critical … lets just get this going and have a defined way to guard against this going forward"*).**

**What forced it.** The operator proposed a tertiary plane-qualified identifier (`[D/M/H]-[plan/engine/doc/tool]-[ID]`) to solve the namespace overlap surfaced by the `E.1.2.B` sweep. Costing it against real citation volume killed the shape and, more usefully, exposed that "doc rot" had been conflated into one problem when it is **three with different fixes**:

| # | Mechanism | Live instance | Guard status before this decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **ID ambiguity** — one citable ID, two meanings, or cited-but-undefined | `M9` claimed twice; `D-1`..`D-13` contradicting across two logs | none → TECH_DEBT-249, `0.2` (e) |
| **2** | **Citation staleness** — a `file:line` pointer to moved / renamed / deleted code | the decision log cites `CoreLatencyStats.hpp:248` + `CoreModelZoo.hpp`, both **gone**; `EngineSharded/Run.hpp` + `SlowPath.hpp` **moved** under `CoreFrameworks/` | **ALREADY DETECTED, IGNORED** — B-Plus prints `146 drift + 4 OOB + 6 missing` on every commit as ADVISORY |
| **3** | **Content staleness** — a doc that is internally consistent but simply dead | the `.E-architecture-v1` log read `status: pre-plan-body` for ~7 weeks after v2 superseded it | none (index-currency covers only the MASTER↔handoff pointer) |

**The identifier scheme is REJECTED, on merit not cost alone.** (a) The **letter already encodes the plane** — `H`=engine invariant, `T`=tool invariant, `Class N`=code anti-pattern, `AR`/`WH`=process anti-pattern, `M`=meta-discipline, `B`=blindspot pillar; `H-engine-21` is redundant. The `T1`-`T12` mint is the precedent done right — plane encoded in the letter at creation. (b) **Plane is METADATA, not IDENTITY: embedding it makes identity MUTABLE.** Reclassifying a decision from doc-plane to tool-plane would rename `D-doc-388`→`D-tool-388` — a citable-identifier rename, i.e. the H21 violation this whole thread exists to prevent. An immutable ID must not contain a mutable attribute. (c) Cost is real and partly **unfixable**: ~21,500 live citations (`D-nnn` 7,677 · `Class N` 5,256 · `TECH_DEBT-nnn` 3,971 · `M1-9` 2,648 · `H20-22` 1,456 · `AR-n` 536), and those inside **commit messages are immutable** — a rename permanently strands them. (d) The overlap it targeted is **global-vs-LOCAL** (`M1`-`M9` = meta-disciplines globally but MEDIUM finding IDs inside audit reports), which the `F-M1` template prefix already fixes at ~zero cost.

**The decisions.**
1. **Mechanism 2 gets teeth by SEVERITY FLIP, not new tooling.** B-Plus already finds it every commit; it is advisory, so it is scrolled past. `MISSING` (file absent) and `OOB` (line past EOF) become **HARD** — both are unambiguously wrong, and there are only ~10. `drift` stays advisory: line numbers legitimately shift and a hard-fail there would train the operator to ignore the gate (the Class-51 credibility failure in reverse).
2. **BASELINE-AND-RED-ON-NEW, per the operator's historical-isn't-critical call.** Grandfather today's staleness into an exception list (the established `tools/lib/*_baseline.txt` pattern, sister to the conversion-completeness HARD-zero flip), fix only the ~10 hard ones, and make any NEW stale citation red. **Explicitly NOT mass-repairing the 146 drift** — a frozen historical record citing a then-real path is a truthful artifact, and chasing it burns budget on the lowest-value rot. The guard's job is to stop the corpus getting WORSE.
3. **Teach the resolver the RENAME MAP before declaring MISSING.** `Core*`→`Node*` is a *documented* terminology migration (DESIGN_PHILOSOPHY § 15 Glossary, the `per-core`→`per-node` evolution) and `CoreLatencyStats.hpp`→`NodeLatencyStats.hpp` is exactly that. A resolver that tries known renames converts most MISSING hits into auto-repairs instead of manual chases. Same for path-prefix moves (`EngineSharded/Run.hpp` → `CoreFrameworks/EngineSharded/Run.hpp`).
4. **Mechanism 3 gets a mechanical supersede check.** Two docs sharing a `plan:` frontmatter value where the older lacks `superseded_by:` is a detectable shape. Cheap, and it closes the 7-week window that let a dead decision log read as live.
5. **`plane:` metadata is DECOUPLED and deprioritized.** Worth having for filtering (`rg '^plane: tool'`), but it is a convenience, not a rot fix — do not let it ride along with the guard work and inflate the increment.

**One resolver, three consumers — why this belongs in `0.2` and not a new lane.** `check_code_tag_blocks.py:155-206` already builds a citable-ID membership index and already resolves `[REFERENCE]`/`[SUPPORTING_DOCS]` citations — but only inside CODE tag-blocks, never prose. Lifting it into `tools/lib/citable_ids.py` **[PATH SUPERSEDED → `tools/citable_ids.py`, see C-389]** serves the **CI gate** (mechanisms 1-3), the **comment TAG system** (`[REFERENCE]` resolution is the same operation), and the **plugin** (a dead `[REFERENCE]` is exactly the dead-link the doc-viewer pop-out at `0.4` must not open). That is the one-core-N-consumers thesis (D-337) applied to citation resolution — not a fourth parallel thing.

**FALSE-POSITIVE SURFACE (discovered empirically the moment this entry was committed — required per M3).** Writing D-390 **raised** the corpus's MISSING count from 6 to 7: this entry cites `CoreLatencyStats.hpp:248` *as its own worked example of a dead citation*, and the detector duly flagged it. **A citation that DOCUMENTS staleness is not staleness** — nor is a citation inside a frozen postmortem, a superseded doc, or a tombstone. The guard MUST distinguish:

- **a citation asserting a fact** ("the logic lives at `X.hpp:248`") — a dead target here is a real defect;
- **a citation mentioning a path as EVIDENCE** ("`X.hpp:248` no longer exists, which is the bug") — a dead target here is the POINT.

Without that distinction the guard red-builds every entry that documents a dead pointer, which makes documenting rot *more expensive than ignoring it* — precisely inverting the incentive, and the fastest way to get a gate disabled. **Implementation options:** exempt code-fenced and blockquoted cites; exempt docs carrying `status: superseded` / `type: postmortem`; or require an explicit inline marker for evidence-cites. Decide at build time with a real corpus pass — but the requirement is settled: **the guard must not punish the act of recording rot.** **DEMONSTRATED, NOT HYPOTHESIZED — the count escalated TWICE while writing this:** committing the entry took the corpus from 6 missing to 7 (the worked-example cite), and committing THIS false-positive-surface paragraph took it from 7 to 10 — the new flags include the *placeholder filename* used inside the sentence explaining that placeholders are not defects. The detector flagged the argument for not flagging it. Three self-demonstrations in one entry, in under an hour, with no reviewer involved. Note also the **monotonic-escalation property**: because documenting the problem enlarges the problem, an un-exempted guard makes each round of honest recording strictly more expensive than the last — the incentive doesn't merely invert, it compounds. Treat the exemption as a BUILD-BLOCKER for (g), not a polish item. Sister: M3 (anti-pattern codification distinguishes legitimate siblings) · Class-51 (a guard whose noise gets it ignored is worth less than no guard).

<!-- D/C/F: C-386 -->
**C-386 — CORRECTION to D-386: "reuse the parity §3b exclusion" cites a COMMENT, not an implementation; no normalizer exists anywhere in the toolchain — 2026-07-19 (found by the `0.2` armed I→A sweep; operator: *"did we reference the wrong thing?"*).** D-386 instructs: *"Normalize the volatile envelope frame (`target.git_head` · `producer.version` · `status`) out of the comparison — reuse the parity §3b exclusion, else every commit REDs."* **There is nothing to reuse.** `parity_check.sh:74-76` comments that *"The volatile envelope frame [git_head/version/status] is EXCLUDED"* — and that is TRUE about the EFFECT: `:84-85` does `pay = env["payload"]`, so the frame is excluded by never being read. It is a **projection, not a normalizer**, and the comment describes an emergent property as though it were a mechanism. Verified: no normalize/redact/scrub machinery exists in any `tools/*.py`, `tools/*.sh`, or the engine's determinism gates. Three disqualifiers for golden reuse: **(1) it does not generalize past an envelope** — `gen_code_map.sh:274` emits `date +%Y-%m-%d` + `git rev-parse --short HEAD` inside MARKDOWN prose, which has no `payload` key to project to; **(2) it is lossy in the wrong direction** — `members()` takes `set(r[0] …)`, column 0 only, order- and duplicate-discarding, which is precisely why `unit_types.closable` needed a separate hand-coded block at `:94-99`; that does not scale to a golden that must pin every column in order; **(3) it discards exactly what a golden should pin** — the frame IS the D-379/D-384 contract. **The fix is small** — a redacting normalizer (replace volatile VALUES with `<GIT_HEAD>`/`<TOOLCHAIN_VERSION>`/`<ENGINE>` sentinels while PINNING `envelope_version`/`kind`/`schema_version`/`payload_schema_version`/`producer.tool`/`status.ok` verbatim). Redaction is strictly stronger than projection: it catches a frame that changes SHAPE (a dropped or renamed key), which projection cannot. **Generalized lesson (the reason this is logged as a correction rather than silently built):** a decision that cites a code COMMENT as its reuse target inherits whatever gap sits between the comment and the code. Cross-ref: D-386 (corrected) · D-384 #1/#2 (the frame contract) · D-382 §5 (never a count-proxy).

<!-- D/C/F: D-391 -->
**D-391 — GUARD ORACLES TERMINATE IN A HUMAN-BLESSED ANCHOR, NEVER IN THE GUARD'S OWN OUTPUT ("anchor, don't layer") — 2026-07-19 (operator: *"doesnt this become a chicken or egg situation … this becomes a recursive spiral does it not? at some point we have to accept manual alignment right?"*).**

**The question that forced it.** Scoping `0.2`'s guard work surfaced an apparent infinite regress: a tool checks the corpus, the tool is in the corpus, so what checks the checker? The operator named it directly and proposed the terminator: at some point, manual alignment.

**The resolution — the fatal shape is narrower than "a tool checking a tool."** It is **a tool being its own ORACLE**. Live instance, verified: `add_vocab.py:85-105` `_regen_and_verify` writes via `rebuild_doc_indexes`, then verifies via `rebuild_doc_indexes --check` — **the same code computes both sides**, so a wrong generator is confirmed by an agreeing checker. That is D-385's partial-oracle shape, standing in the tree. By contrast a tool compared against a **frozen artifact a human blessed** is NOT circular: the anchor is external and the regress terminates there. This codebase already carries both terminator forms — **T5's synthetic planted fixture** (the known-bad half) and **D-386's blessed golden** (the known-good half). Neither needed inventing; they needed *naming as the terminator*.

**The diagnosis this flips.** The toolchain's problem is NOT too few guard layers — it is that the existing guards are **UNANCHORED**, and each was found green while asserting nothing: `check_code_tag_blocks.py:170` builds membership by MENTION (a set that admits anything it has seen cannot go red) · `toolio.py` `read(env): return env["payload"]` discards the envelope frame, so all three D-379 version axes are validated by nothing (proven: an envelope with 9 simultaneous frame corruptions passes §3b) · `parity_check.sh:35-37` compares exit codes for EQUALITY, never zero, so both-sides-failing prints `PARITY: PASS`. **Adding another unanchored guard makes this worse, not safer.**

**The rule.** Every guard terminates in exactly ONE human-blessed anchor (a golden, or a synthetic planted fixture). No guard's oracle is its own output. **Corollary — freeze ADDING guard layers until the existing ones are anchored** (NOT "freeze CI": the standing HARD checks are load-bearing and stay). **Why this SHRINKS `0.2` rather than growing it:** anchoring is bounded work per guard, whereas layering compounds — and it is `feedback_structural_fix_over_belt_and_suspenders` applied to the verification substrate itself (remove the special-case, don't add a redundant layer).

**Relationship to T5 — deliberately left open, under adversarial test.** T5 states *"every guard asserts its OWN non-vacuity (planted-bad REDs and known-good passes)."* If this collapses into T5, codifying it separately is the parallel-infrastructure anti-pattern D-388 deliberately avoided; the candidate residue is that T5 governs the guard's TEETH while this governs its ORACLE'S INDEPENDENCE. **Not codified as `T13` until the a-class verdict lands.** Cross-ref: D-385/M10 (partial vs total oracle — this is its structural generalization) · D-386 (the golden = the anchor) · T5 · D-362 (frozen synthetic calibration fixtures) · `feedback_structural_fix_over_belt_and_suspenders`.
<!-- STATUS: **REFUTED + WITHDRAWN** (a-class A-1, 2026-07-19 — verdict REFUTED; orchestrator independently verified the two load-bearing counter-claims and CONCEDES). NOT codified. No `T13`. See C-391 immediately below for the reversal and what survives. -->

<!-- D/C/F: C-391 -->
**C-391 — REVERSAL of D-391: the "anchor, don't layer" principle is REFUTED and WITHDRAWN; its surviving residue was already codified SEVEN WEEKS earlier — 2026-07-19 (a-class A-1; orchestrator concedes on independent verification).**

**The decisive refutation — the canonical sister already exists.** `memory/feedback_golden_master_over_reimplemented_oracle.md` (**D-74, established 2026-05-29**) already states D-391's entire positive half, near-verbatim: *"run the REAL path X on fixed input, **freeze the exact output once, diff every run against it**… The golden-master has no parallel implementation to drift: the **'expected' IS the real output; a regression is a diff, not a judgment**… An *intentional* change means a **deliberate, reviewed golden regeneration**."* **D-386 already cites this memory by name** (`:2303`). The input-side dual is likewise already numbered: **T2** (grammar DERIVED from a human-authored fence, never hardcoded) + **T10** (schema-as-DATA registry read by both implementations). D-391 was the output-axis mirror of T2/T10 and a fourth restatement of D-74. Minting it as `T13` would be **precisely** the parallel-infrastructure shape D-388 refused eleven decisions earlier (*"One fix, three deliberate non-builds"*), and precisely what `feedback_audit_canonical_sister_before_new_infra` exists to catch. **Overlap on the positive half: ~100%.** The residue T5 does not cover is real — T5 governs a guard's TEETH, D-74 governs its ORACLE'S PROVENANCE — but that residue has a home and did not need a new one.

**The kill-shot landed: three UN-ANCHORABLE guards inside `0.2`, two of them PRE-CONCEDED in decided entries.** (1) **The §11.5 codification-coverage checker** — `DESIGN_PHILOSOPHY.md` §11.5's M10 row already says *"CI stub deliberately OMITTED — oracle totality is a judgment about what a check covers, not a parseable property"*, and the required-site set is **non-uniform BY DESIGN** (M3 legitimately has no spec and no memory; M9 is legitimately PARTIAL per TECH_DEBT-248). Any "expected sites" table you could bless IS the judgment the checker was supposed to make, relocated. (2) **D-390's assert-vs-evidence citation distinction** — authorial INTENT; all three proposed proxies are heuristics, none a blessed artifact (and the orchestrator empirically confirmed one of them fails: a `>` blockquote does NOT exempt, see D-392). (3) **D-389 decision 3** — namespace-scope overlap, *explicitly de-mechanized* (*"convention where mechanization would lie"*). **D-391 is therefore incomplete as stated**; narrowing it to "mechanically-decidable properties" collapses it into D-386.

**The diagnosis was FALSE, and self-contradictory.** D-391 asserted *"the problem is NOT too few guard layers."* But D-389 records *"nothing enforces that meaning is unique, defined, or reachable"* and D-390's table records mechanism 3's guard status as **"none"** — those are **coverage gaps (zero guards), not unanchored guards**. The distinction D-391 failed to draw is coverage-gap vs layering, and as written its "freeze adding guard layers" corollary would have **frozen its own increment**: (e) and (g) ARE new guards over unguarded properties, both `DECIDED` on operator rationale (*"workspace debt … leads to dated and rotted information, and we cant make decisions with that"*).

**The cost inverted.** `DOCS/TOOLS.md` carries ~101 tool rows, **38 STANDING-CI**. Blanket-anchoring is ~42 goldens with per-tool normalization + bless paths — versus D-386's already-decided ~8 highest-fan-out targets. D-391's prescription was ~5× the decided scope, so its `feedback_structural_fix_over_belt_and_suspenders` justification ran backwards: it ADDED moving parts.

**The regress does not terminate where claimed, and the taxonomy omitted the terminator that works.** (a) *A blessing is a signature, not a verification* — `parity_check.sh` compares a 1832-row inventory; no human verifies 1832 rows, so by M10's own taxonomy a human-blessed golden is itself a **PARTIAL** oracle. D-385's recorded self-demonstration (its author producing a FALSE finding while documenting the discipline) is the proof. The honest form is weaker and true: *anchoring converts an unbounded self-referential regress into a BOUNDED one terminating in reviewable diffs.* (b) **The omitted third terminator is INDEPENDENT ADVERSARIAL REVIEW.** `RECURRING_BUG_PATTERNS.md:102` (Class-51's meta-lesson): *"the BUILDER is MODEL-BOUNDED … vacuously-green guards are caught by INDEPENDENT adversarial review NOT the builder's dogfood."* That is **T11/D-383**, and it found *every defect in D-391's own evidence list* — the M9 collision, the D-1..D-13 split, the 8 dangling IDs, D-385's false finding. **Not one was found by an anchor.** D-391 proposed elevating a mechanism that caught none of them over the one that caught all of them — and this very reversal is that mechanism working.

**ORCHESTRATOR CORRECTIONS to over-claims made while proposing D-391** (recorded because they were relayed to the operator with more confidence than they earned): (i) **`add_vocab.py` is NOT wholly circular** — VERIFIED: `:91-99` performs genuinely INDEPENDENT cross-module derivation checks (`check_code_tag_blocks.load_categories` + `check_doc_metadata.load_vocabulary`, both different modules from the writer). Only `:100-103` (the post-regen `--check` re-run) is circular, and only *in that calling context*. The original "same code computes both sides" characterization was too strong. (ii) **`check_code_tag_blocks.py`'s membership sets are LOOSE, not vacuous** — 2 of 5 are already definition-anchored (`:162-163` DECISION via the defining `<!-- D/C/F -->` sentinel; `:185` PARITY via anchored `^id: PARITY-`). "A set that cannot go red" is true of the TECH_DEBT/CLASS legs only; an unmentioned id still REDs. (iii) **`parity_check.sh`'s equality-not-zero check is the CORRECT division of labour TODAY** — proven by planting an invalid `[TAG]`: `parity_check.sh` printed `PARITY: PASS` while `check_session_docs.sh` went `❌ SWEEP FAILED` on the same corpus. The gate's contract is *agreement*; *validity* is held elsewhere. It is not a defect today — see F-393.

**What survives, and where it already lives:** *for mechanically-decidable OUTPUT properties, prefer a fixed-input frozen artifact with an explicit diff-showing re-bless over any check whose reference is the checker's own re-derivation.* That sentence is `feedback_golden_master_over_reimplemented_oracle`, scheduled as **D-386 / deliverable (b)**. Nothing further is minted. Cross-ref: D-391 (withdrawn) · D-74/`feedback_golden_master_over_reimplemented_oracle` (the pre-existing home) · T2/T10 · D-386 · D-388 (the non-forking precedent) · T11/D-383 (the terminator that actually works) · Class-51 meta-lesson · `feedback_audit_canonical_sister_before_new_infra`.

<!-- D/C/F: F-393 -->
**F-393 — FORWARD / BUILD-BLOCKER for `0.2` (c): wiring `parity_check.sh` as the tools-gate WIDENS its contract from AGREEMENT to VALIDITY, and it was never written for that.** The one genuinely NEW actionable finding from the A-1 pass (it is a cascade, not an anchoring problem — and D-391 would not have surfaced it). **Today** the division is correct: `parity_check.sh` asserts the two implementations AGREE; `check_session_docs.sh` asserts the corpus is VALID. Proven by planting an invalid `[TAG]` — parity printed `PARITY: PASS` (exit 0) while the doc sweep went `❌ SWEEP FAILED`. **At D-387 decision (3)** — decided, scoped into `0.2` (c) — `parity_check.sh` becomes the WIRED tools-gate, and its exit code starts carrying a validity contract it does not implement: `:35-37` asserts only `PY_RC == CXX_RC`, so **two implementations agreeing the corpus is broken will gate a commit GREEN.** Compounding: §3b/§4 carry non-emptiness asserts (`:132-133`, `:160`) but **§1 and §2 do not** (both-empty diffs read as identical), and `build.sh` at `:17` is unchecked (`set -uo pipefail`, no `-e`). **Required before wiring:** (i) a VALIDITY leg distinct from the equality leg (`PY_RC == CXX_RC` **and** `PY_RC == 0`); (ii) non-emptiness asserts on the §1/§2 diffs, matching the pattern already at `:132-133`/`:160`; (iii) check `build.sh`'s exit status. Also queued from the same pass: `add_vocab.py:100-103`'s post-regen `--check` is vacuous in context — drop it or move it BEFORE the regen. Cross-ref: D-387 (3) · C-391 · Class-51 · T5.

<!-- D/C/F: C-392 -->
**C-392 — CORRECTIONS to D-392 from the a-class A-3 pass: (e) is NOT a lockstep two-implementation change (REFUTED EMPIRICALLY), plus four remedy corrections — 2026-07-19.** A-3 refuted the load-bearing claim by RUNNING the change rather than reasoning about it; the orchestrator re-ran it independently and CONCEDES.

1. **⭐ (e) IS NOT LOCKSTEP — REFUTED, and D-392 item 1 is WRONG.** Hardening the Python membership sets from by-MENTION to definition-anchored does **not** red parity. Measured independently (orchestrator re-run, not relayed): **TECH_DEBT by-mention 240 → definition-anchored 239 — exactly ONE id drops, `TECH_DEBT-101`, which is `[REFERENCE]`d from ZERO engine source files** (`rg` over all `.hpp`/`.cpp`: 0 hits). **CLASS: 56 → 56, zero drop. PARITY: already definition-anchored** at `check_code_tag_blocks.py:185` (`^id:\s*PARITY-`). Every TECH_DEBT id actually cited from a code tag-block (23, 40, 154, 160, 182, 192) and every cited Class (18, 23, 26, 38) resolves in BOTH sets. Therefore `validate`'s output is **byte-unchanged**, the §1 violations diff stays 0 lines, and **Python can be hardened ALONE with parity green.**
   - **The error, named precisely (worth more than the finding):** the STRUCTURE was verified correctly — the C++ twin genuinely exists (`foxtag.hpp:406`/`:432`/`:583` → `:766`), it genuinely feeds `validate`, and `parity_check.sh:20-21` genuinely diffs `validate`. **The CONSEQUENCE was assumed, not measured.** Whether hardening changes `validate`'s OUTPUT is an empirical question that a 20-line script answers. Structural verification is not behavioural verification — `feedback_passing_test_is_not_verification` applied to one's own verification step.
   - **This also falsifies the PLAN BODY's own prerequisite.** `subplans/2026-07-19-E.1.2.B-toolchain-v1-plugin-first.md` (e) states *"fix the by-mention membership sets FIRST (prerequisite; **it will newly-RED existing by-mention refs** — enumerate them before flipping)."* On the code-tag-block surface it newly-REDs **nothing**; the enumeration it asks for returns `{TECH_DEBT-101}`, uncited from code. Plan text corrected in the same commit.
   - **Narrow residue where lockstep IS genuine:** (i) changing `_ref_resolves` NORMALIZATION semantics (`check_code_tag_blocks.py:191-206` vs `foxtag.hpp:583-600`); (ii) adding a membership index for a newly-fenced subcat that is cited-and-dangling in a code block. Adding a subcat ROW alone is parity-safe — both sides derive `ref_subcats` from the fence at runtime and degrade identically to existence-unchecked.
   - **The shared-registry remedy is REFUTED as over-engineering that ADDS a parity hazard.** `toolio_schemas.json` is a COLUMN-NAME registry (declarative field lists, trivially portable). A ref-index registry must carry source paths, **extraction regexes**, and normalization rules — but **foxtag contains ZERO `std::regex`** (grepped both files: 0 hits); it hand-parses character-by-character (`foxtag.hpp:440-449`, `:453-470`). Making C++ consume Python-flavoured regexes means adding a regex engine and proving byte-identical semantics — introducing parity risk exactly where parity is currently proven green. **Correct shape: extract + harden Python-side (parity-green by construction), leave the C++ twin untouched, and touch it only when residue (i) or (ii) fires — gated by `parity_check.sh` as D-349/T4 already require.**
2. **`--bless`: placement SURVIVES, the confirmation gate is WITHDRAWN.** D-392 item 6 invented *"exits non-zero unless explicitly confirmed."* **Zero precedent exists:** no `input(` in any `tools/*.py`, no `read -p` in any `tools/*.sh`, corpus-wide. Both real sisters write and return 0 (`check_identifier_retirement.py:277-281` `--update`; `check_latency_path_conformance.py:569` `--update-budgets`). **The sisters' actual contract is that THE FLAG IS THE CONFIRMATION** — two disjoint modes, verify (exit 1, name the flag) or write (exit 0). A prompt would block mid-run and make D-374 not one action. **Keep "shows the diff" (D-386 requires it); drop the prompt and the non-zero exit — showing a diff is not blocking on it.** Also CORRECTED: `check_fp_determinism.sh` was miscited as a bless sister — it has **no** bless flag, only a manual `g++` recipe in a comment at `:13-15`. The evidence was 2-of-3, not 3-of-3.
   - **The stronger argument D-392 failed to state:** D-374 requires the pipeline be IDEMPOTENT (0-diff on re-run). An auto-blessing orchestrator makes the golden match BY CONSTRUCTION — a vacuously-green gate, Class-51, exactly what **T5** forbids. That, not sister-symmetry, is why bless must not sit inside the orchestrator loop.
3. **D-374 stage 2: RECLASSIFY to the verify phase, do NOT delete.** D-392 item 4 over-corrected. `add_vocab.py`'s contract (`DOCS/TOOLS.md:53`) is *"insert a vocab tag into its SSoT + regen the indexes + **verify it derives**"* — the grammar stage's real job is the **T2 propagation PROOF**, not a write. Deleting it removes the only check that a fence edit actually reached every consumer, which is the whole point of grammar-DERIVED-never-hardcoded.
4. **D-374 stage 4: the canonical sister was MISSED — headless-nvim, not C++ promotion and not drop.** `check_cache_layout.py:77` **already drives Lua headlessly from the CLI**: `["nvim","--headless","--clean","-u","NONE","-l",str(EMITTER),tu]`. The same mechanism can drive `tagwriter.lua`. An included stage already writes there, so gitignored cannot be the exclusion criterion. Calibration note: `DOCS/TOOLS.md:98` says CODE_MAP is already "regen-on-use" — but covered by a remembered ritual spread across two skills, which is precisely what D-374 exists to replace.
6. **C-389 placement SURVIVES — but NARROW the rationale, and it has TWO more contradicting sites.** Leading with guard-blindness is leading with the CONSEQUENCE; if the globs were ever hardened the blindness argument evaporates while the right answer does not change, and *an argument that can be invalidated without changing its conclusion is the wrong argument to hand an implementer.* **Lead instead with D-384's data/module TIER convention + the `foxroots.py`/`toolio.py` precedent** (both importable libraries with multiple consumers, both at module tier, both dispositioned `LIBRARY (imported) + runnable`). Also: recursing the globs was independently REFUTED on cascade — it immediately REDs 5 nested scripts, two of which live in the SEPARATE plugin repo, and `check_tools_inventory.py:31`'s `ROW_RE` forbids `/` so 3 slash-bearing rows already fail to parse. **`tools/lib/citable_ids.py` appears at THREE sites** — D-389 dec. 2, D-390, and the plan body (e) — all authored the same day, all postdating D-384. C-389 corrected the first; **the other two need the same correction or an implementer follows the later, more-specific text straight into the blind spot.**
7. **NEW BUILD-BLOCKER — `registry_id:` is a vacuity trap for (e)'s own guard.** D-389 decision 4 makes `registry_id:` "the parse surface" while recording it has **zero consumers at 2-of-192 adoption**. A guard keyed off it is **vacuously green on 190 specs** — Class-51 inside the guard built to close a Class-51 hole. <!-- D/C/F: D-393 -->
**D-393 — THE CORPUS CONTRACT: corpus membership is SINGLE-SOURCED, and `.gitignore` is NEVER an input to it — 2026-07-19 (operator, rejecting a two-option patch framing: *"these both seem like incomplete ideas, that dont really fit in with the unified core and stuff"*).**

**What forced it.** Build-blocker 1 (the vacuous-green: `foxtag validate <dir>` → rc=0 *"All tag-blocks valid"* with 5 tagged files present; a MISSING path → rc=0 on BOTH tools) was presented as a fork between (a) make foxtag match Python's rc=1 and (b) make both expand directories. **The operator rejected both as patches that do not fit the one-core thesis, and was right:** both options are *synchronize two implementations*, which is exactly what T1/T10 exist to eliminate.

**The actual defect: THREE enumerators, none authoritative.** (1) `check_code_tag_blocks.py:263` `engine_source_files()` — `ENGINE.rglob("*.hpp") + rglob("*.cpp")`, excludes `vendor`/`build*`, adds `tests/schema_golden`; **UNSORTED** for the engine portion (a cross-machine determinism hazard). (2) `check_conversion_completeness.py:158` — `subprocess rg -l --glob '*.hpp' --glob '*.cpp' '^// \[SCHEMA\]_\[v1'`, i.e. **gitignore-blind (TECH_DEBT-245)** because `rg` respects `.gitignore` by default. (3) `foxtag.hpp:790` `scan_files(roots)` — C++, sorted. Three definitions of "what is the corpus," disagreeing on ordering, on gitignored files, and on path-argument semantics.

**THE DECISION.**
1. **Corpus membership is single-sourced as a CONTRACT read by every consumer** — roots (already `foxroots`) · extensions · an EXPLICIT exclusion list (`vendor`, `build*`) · extra roots (`tests/schema_golden`) · bytewise sort · the anchored converted-file selector (`^// \[SCHEMA\]_\[v1`) · **path-argument semantics: a DIRECTORY expands by the same rules; a MISSING path LOUD-FAILS rc=2 on every implementation.** The vacuous-green then *falls out of the contract* rather than being patched into each tool.
2. **`.gitignore` is NEVER an implicit input to corpus membership.** Gitignore is a **DISTRIBUTION** concern; corpus membership is a **TOOLING** concern. Nobody decided to couple them — it is a side effect of `rg`'s default. TECH_DEBT-245 therefore closes **structurally, not by policy**: the enumerator stops consulting gitignore at all, and exclusions become explicit. *(The operator's all-private pivot independently kills the other half: source was gitignored-in-place to keep a public repo code-only, and that rationale is dead — see the SUPERSEDED banner on `public-private-boundary-and-ecosystem-discipline.md`. Any surviving ignore must be justified by a CURRENT reason — build artifacts, generated files, secrets — never by corpus intent.)*
3. **This is NOT new infrastructure — it COMPLETES a half-done extraction.** `engine_source_files()` already exists, is already exported, and already has two consumers (`rebuild_doc_indexes.py` + its own tool); `DOCS/TOOLS.md:41` already describes it as *"the code-tag-index collector — **one grammar, one file-list**."* The defect is that `check_conversion_completeness` bypasses it with raw `rg` and foxtag carries an independent `scan_files`. Canonical-sister discipline satisfied by EXTENDING the existing helper, not minting a parallel one.
4. **Why A-3's "shared registry = over-engineering" objection does NOT transfer.** A-3 correctly refuted a shared registry for the REF-INDEX, because that registry would have to carry extraction **REGEXES** and **foxtag contains zero `std::regex`** — it hand-parses char-by-char (`foxtag.hpp:440-449`), so importing Python-flavoured regex semantics would add a regex engine and *create* parity risk. **A corpus contract carries extensions, path globs, a prefix anchor, and a sort rule — all trivially portable declarative data.** That is the `toolio_schemas.json` shape (D-380 schema-as-DATA), not the ref-index shape. The distinction is the difference between the right structural move and the one just refuted.
5. **Sequencing honours T4/D-349.** Single-source the RULES now (data both sides read); **Python stays CI-AUTHORITATIVE.** Promoting the enumerator into the C++ core as a `foxtag corpus --json` PRODUCER is the declared **convergence target** — "what files are in the corpus" is a FACT per D-384's fact-vs-workflow boundary, and D-378 already catalogs payload kinds — but it is a gated cutover behind `parity_check.sh`, homed at `0.7`, NOT this increment.

**What ONE fix closes:** the vacuous-green (build-blocker 1) · the T1 directory-arg divergence (Python rc=1 vs foxtag rc=0) · the **common-mode** missing-path hole (both rc=0 — parity is structurally blind to it, so only a contract or a golden catches it) · **TECH_DEBT-245** (gitignore blindness) · the unsorted-vs-sorted cross-machine hazard · and the ANCHORED-selector gotcha that produced a **false finding** during `0.1.5`. TECH_DEBT-246 (FUNCTIONS never checked) is adjacent — the shared enumerator is its precondition, not its fix.

**C-395 — CORRECTION to D-393: extending the enumerator alone does NOT close build-blocker 1 (the `--paths` branch bypasses it), and there are SIX enumerators in TWO families, not three — 2026-07-19 (scoped i-class re-sweep per T11/D-383; all four claims independently re-verified by the orchestrator).**

1. **⭐ THE LOAD-BEARING CORRECTION — D-393 pt 3 names the wrong seam.** D-393 says *"EXTEND the existing `engine_source_files()`."* **The vacuous-green does not live there.** It lives on the path-ARGUMENT branch, which never calls the enumerator at all — verified verbatim:
   ```python
   # check_code_tag_blocks.py:598-601
   if args.paths: files = [Path(p) for p in args.paths]   # ← the vacuous branch
   else:          files = engine_source_files()            # ← the branch D-393 names
   ```
   Same shape in the C++ twin (`foxtag_main.cpp:32`, `paths.empty() ? scan_files(roots) : paths`), and the silent-skip itself is one line below at `:37` — `if (!fs::exists(f, ec)) continue;`. A faithful implementation of D-393 pt 3 would have landed a real fix and left **build-blocker 1 fully open.** **The contract MUST also export a path RESOLVER (`resolve_paths()`), and the `--paths` branches must route through it** (`check_code_tag_blocks.py:598`, `check_cache_layout.py:438`, `foxtag_main.cpp:32`). *(This is the C-392 lesson recurring: the structure was right, the consequence was assumed. D-393 correctly identified that membership was multi-sourced; it wrongly inferred that the enumerator was therefore the fix site.)*
2. **SIX enumerators in TWO exclusion families.** Beyond the three D-393 names, verified: `check_cache_layout.py:442` (inline), `check_register_fit.py:108` `corpus_files()`, `check_schema_version.py:72` `corpus_files()` — each a re-copy adding `schema_golden` + `DOCS` to the exclusion set. So a FLAT one-list contract cannot serve them and they would stay Class-18 mirrors. **The contract needs PROFILES** — measured: family A (validate) = 202 files, family B (derived_facts) = 194, **B ⊂ A exactly**, `A−B` = the 8 template/fixture files that legitimately carry illustrative `[DERIVED]` and must not be gated against compiled reality.
3. **The gitignore decoupling's blast radius is exactly ONE file.** Measured: `rg` default 163 → `rg --no-ignore` 164; the single newly-visible file is **`Strategies/private/EmaCross.hpp`** (gitignored at `.gitignore:167`, header `// PRIVATE — do not publish`, already in `engine_source_files()` today, already converted). **TECH_DEBT-245's entire live exposure is one file.** Run it through `check_conversion_completeness` BEFORE the decoupling lands — if it carries a C1/C2/C3 gap the HARD gate REDs on the very commit that lands the contract. One `--write-baseline` regen mitigates, but it must be planned, not discovered.
4. **`rc=2` on a missing path is mechanically FREE.** Verified: **every** CI invocation is path-argument-free (all 8 `check_session_docs.sh` call sites are bare; `.githooks/pre-commit:329` passes `--paths` to a DIFFERENT tool, `check_doc_metadata`, from a git-reported — therefore existing — list). The only path-passing sites are per-file human/agent commands in skills + handoffs, every one of which intends an existing file. **No caller legitimately passes a missing path**, so the loud-fail breaks nothing. `rc=2` is also the established loud-fail code on this surface (`check_code_tag_blocks.py:592,608` · `check_conversion_completeness.py:215` · `foxtag_main.cpp:157,161,165,174`) — no new convention is invented.
5. **A THIRD live Class-51, in a wired HARD gate — `check_schema_version.py` scans ZERO `.py` files.** Its docstring (`:72-75`) states `.py` is included *"so a TOOL-fixture drift (the origin of the 2026-07-18 incident) is caught too."* Measured: `ENGINE.rglob("*.py")` returns **exactly one** file (`vendor/imgui/misc/debuggers/imgui_lldb.py`), which the `vendor` filter then drops → **0 scanned**. The 61 real tool `.py` files live under `tools/`, a **directory SYMLINK**, which `rglob` does not descend. It is wired HARD at `check_session_docs.sh:110`. **Do NOT naively "fix" it:** adding `tools/` as a root yields 12 non-locked matches across 4 files, nearly all false positives from the checkers' own detection source (a checker matching its own format string). Correct fix needs the `#`-comment anchored selector from the multi-comment-syntax north-star plus self-exclusion. **TOMBSTONED in the contract + TECH_DEBT-253 — recorded as OPEN, never as closed.**
6. **Contract shape confirmed implementable with ZERO new readers.** JSON at `tools/lib/corpus_contract.json` (DATA tier per C-389), read by foxtag's already-promoted hand-rolled `json_parse` — the exact two-reader model `toolio_schemas.json` already proves in-tree (`foxtag_main.cpp:163-175`). Declarative only (extensions · path-part exclusions · extra roots · a line-prefix anchor · a sort rule) — **no regexes**, so the C-392 refutation does not recur. Deliberately a SEPARATE file from `toolio_schemas.json`: that registry is keyed by emitted-payload KIND, whereas corpus membership is an INPUT rule; merging them would poison the `corpus/1` output row that the `0.7` producer will earn.

<!-- D/C/F: C-396 -->
**C-396 — a-class verdict on D-393/C-395: SURVIVES-NARROWED. One real silently-dependent consumer found (with its non-vacuity proof), a MISSING symlink clause, and a sort clause that is phantom/ungated/hazardous — 2026-07-19.**

1. **⭐ THE SILENTLY-DEPENDENT CONSUMER — found, and it is the profiles requirement's non-vacuity PROOF.** `check_cache_layout.py:442-446` **deliberately** excludes `DOCS` and `schema_golden`, documented in-source: *"DOCS/ = template corpus etc. — illustrative `[STRUCT]` DERIVED on non-compiled copy-source (sister to the schema_golden exclusion)."* Collapse the exclusion sets into one flat list and this **STANDING-CI gate REDs immediately** — measured: `[size-drift] ExecutionCore: [SIZE] tag says 192B but real sizeof = 68352B` + `CfgFieldDescriptor: 128B vs 104B`. **The cascade if profiles were dropped as "over-engineering":** the natural fix looks like refreshing the `[SIZE]` tags — which writes **68352B into a copy-paste TEMPLATE**, propagating a wrong derived fact into every future conversion. Class-18 through the front door, on the one-producer-N-consumers surface. C-395 item 2 asserted profiles were needed; this is the evidence it lacked. **Do not let profiles get trimmed during implementation.**
2. **⭐ NEW — the contract has NO SYMLINK CLAUSE, and symlinks are the DOMINANT divergence axis here, larger than gitignore.** C-395 item 3 measured the gitignore delta as 1 file by holding `rg`'s other behaviours constant. The real enumerator swap is **+9, not +1**: the 1 gitignored file · **3 file-symlinked `DOCS/*TEMPLATE*.hpp`** · 5 `schema_golden` files via a dir symlink. Verified topology: `DOCS/CODE_TAG_TEMPLATES.hpp`, `STRATEGY_TEMPLATE.hpp`, `STRATEGY_BANDIT_TEMPLATE.hpp` are **FILE symlinks** into the workspace; `tools`, `tests`, `plans` are **DIR symlinks**; `DOCS` is a real dir. Three enumerators, three unwritten semantics: `rg` follows **neither** without `-L` (measured: sees **0** `.hpp` in `DOCS/` where `rglob` sees **3**) · Python `rglob` yields file symlinks but does **not** descend dir symlinks · foxtag mirrors Python. **This is the ROOT CAUSE of TECH_DEBT-253** — the 61 tool `.py` are unreachable because `tools/` is a dir symlink; C-395 diagnosed the symptom and tombstoned it without generalising. **The contract needs `follow_file_symlinks` / `follow_dir_symlinks` as explicit per-profile declarative booleans** — trivially portable, and they make the `tests/schema_golden` extra-root hack unnecessary. Without this the contract leaves the exact divergence class it exists to close, and re-mints it at the `0.7` cutover.
3. **NEW — the bytewise-sort clause is PHANTOM, UNGATED, and as worded ACTIVELY HAZARDOUS.** *Phantom:* every observable consumer already sorts (`parity_check.sh:24-25,49` · `foxtag_main.cpp:122` · `rebuild_doc_indexes.py:346-368`), and the validator header is counts-only — **there is no live cross-machine ordering hazard**, so D-393's claim to close one is UNEARNED. *Ungated:* both parity legs sort BEFORE diffing, so no gate can ever verify the two implementations agree on order — a T5 problem inside a T5 fix. *Hazardous:* the corpus spans **two absolute roots**, so a naive whole-list bytewise sort makes the interleave depend on checkout layout (reachable — both tools honour `FOXML_ENGINE`/`FOXML_WORKSPACE`). Today's stability comes from **structural grouping** (engine-then-golden), not lexicography. **Specify: sort WITHIN each root, then concatenate in declared root order — never a whole-list sort over absolute paths — and record that it is CONVENTION, not enforcement.** *(Conceded on the comparator itself: `std::char_traits<char>::lt` compares as `unsigned char` and UTF-8 preserves code-point order, so C++ `<` and Python `sorted()` do agree bytewise. The hazard is the two-root scope, not the comparator.)*
4. **NEW (M9) — FOUR missing-path behaviours, not two.** D-393 said *"rc=0 on BOTH."* Measured: `foxtag validate` rc=0 silent · `check_code_tag_blocks --paths` rc=0 silent · **`check_conversion_completeness --paths` rc=1 via an uncaught `FileNotFoundError` traceback** · `check_cache_layout --paths` rc=0 "gate inert." Harmless (no caller), but it is a categorical claim made over an unenumerated set — **`feedback_enumerate_set_before_categorical_claim` (M9/AR-1), by me, in the entry arguing for rigour.**
5. **Attack 2 REFUTED cleanly — "gitignore is NEVER an input" is CORRECT.** 29 gitignored files sit inside the contract walk: 1 real private source · 3 template-corpus files · 25 inert `experiments/` files with no `[SCHEMA]` block · **ZERO build junk, ZERO backups.** So the explicit exclusion list does NOT have to reproduce `.gitignore`, and no Class-18 mirror is created. **The strongest evidence, which D-393 never cited:** `engine_source_files()` has ALREADY been running gitignore-free over the whole tree the entire time (`:275-278`) — the coupling exists at exactly ONE site, `check_conversion_completeness.py:158`.
6. **Attack 6 SPLIT — the work is justified, the FRAMING is now false.** C-395 proved the one-line `rc` patch would not have closed the blocker at the named seam, and six enumerators are real. **But D-393 pt 3's *"This is NOT new infrastructure — it COMPLETES a half-done extraction"* is FALSE** once profiles + `resolve_paths()` + symlink semantics + a JSON data file + two language readers land. **Keep the work; RETIRE the framing** — it sets the wrong expectation for sequencing and review depth.
7. **Profiles must project EXTENSIONS, not only exclusions** — `check_schema_version.corpus_files()` also needs `.py`.
8. **An open action was CLOSED for us.** C-395 item 3 required running `Strategies/private/EmaCross.hpp` through the completeness gate before the decoupling lands. Done over the full 172-file contract corpus: **0 findings (C1 0 · C2 0 · C3 0)**, including all 9 newly-visible files. `completeness_baseline.txt` is at HARD-zero. **No `--write-baseline` regen needed.**

<!-- D/C/F: C-393 -->
**C-393 — REVERSAL of D-392 item 3: the frozen fixture corpus is DROPPED from `0.2`; the operator's own condition on it was falsified — 2026-07-19 (a-class A-2; orchestrator independently re-verified and CONCEDES).** The operator approved the scope increase conditionally — *"scope increase is fine here **assuming it helps keep everything in check**."* **The assumption does not hold**, so dropping it honours the condition rather than overriding it.

1. **⭐ THE FIXTURES ARE NOT SYNTHETIC — they are transitively LIVE. Refutation in one fact.** `tests/schema_golden/golden_struct_hot.hpp` carries **9 `#include`s** and `golden_file_header.hpp` **20**, pulling in `FixedPointN.hpp`/`NodeLatencyStats.hpp`/`GateParameters.hpp`/`ParameterSlot.hpp`/`SPSCRing.hpp`/`Tick.hpp`/`TradeEvent.hpp`. Verified by direct probe: `foxtag layout tests/schema_golden/golden_struct_hot.hpp` returns **`tt::ExecutionCore<64>: size 68352`** — the LIVE engine's struct. **A "fixture" golden would pin live engine layouts**, i.e. it is a corpus golden wearing a fixture label. The premise that a fixture decouples goldens from corpus volatility is false for the fixtures that exist.
2. **It scores 0 of 3 on the defects `0.2` is chasing — and one is structurally unreachable.** (i) **by-mention membership set: IMPOSSIBLE by construction** — `_build_ref_index()` reads the WORKSPACE DOC LEDGERS (CLAUDE.md, DESIGN_SPECS/, memory/, TECH_DEBT, PARITY, plans/), **not the scanned corpus at all**; swapping the corpus for a fixture changes the scanned set and leaves the defective index byte-identical. (ii) **discarded envelope frame** — a golden that normalizes the frame OUT cannot govern the frame, on any input. (iii) **the exit-code check** is consumer-side, not stdout, so no output golden reaches it.
3. **It has ALREADY drifted, measurably.** `golden_file_header.hpp:13` declares `[FILE]_[CoreFrameworks/EngineCommon.hpp]` with `[ENGINE] [HELPER] [BACKTEST]`; the live file carries `[BACKTEST] [BOOT_TIME] [CFG_FLOW] [ENGINE] [ML_INFERENCE] [SLOW_PATH]`. **Jaccard 2/7 = 29%.** Fixture committed 2026-07-15, live file modified 2026-07-16 — **stale one day after being frozen, unnoticed for four.** Coverage: 5 of 202 files, 5 of 799 tag-blocks (**99.4% unexercised**), 24 of 35 `[TAG]` values absent (including the entire PLANE axis, T3's first-class gating axis), and **ZERO real `[DERIVED]` facts** — every one is a literal placeholder (`[SIZE]_[pending-generator]`).
4. **It contradicts its own governing spec.** `calibration-corpus-non-vacuity-discipline.md` (D-362) mandates the calibration corpus **GROWS** with the discovered error taxonomy. A byte-exact golden over a substrate whose spec requires growth re-blesses on every taxonomy addition. "Frozen" and "grows" cannot both hold.
5. **It would self-invalidate mid-increment.** The fixtures carry live-resolving `[REFERENCE]` ids (`[INVARIANT]_[H17]`, `[PARITY]_[PARITY-031]`, `[FUTURE_WORK]_[TECH_DEBT-169]`) that resolve against the very ledgers `0.2` **(f)** repairs. **A fixture golden blessed at the start of `0.2` goes RED mid-`0.2` because of `0.2`'s own ledger work.**
6. **ORCHESTRATOR GROUNDING CORRECTIONS (both relayed to the operator with unearned confidence):** (i) **The compared header is only HALF corpus-volatile** — `59 concern + 33 surface` is VOCAB-derived and invariant under corpus change; only `Scanned N files; M tag-blocks` moves. D-392 item 2 called it "pure corpus counts." (ii) **`layout`/`fields` do NOT carry `/usr/include` paths in record keys** (measured: 0 hits) — the keys are type names. The real cross-machine hazard there is ABI-derived VALUES (e.g. `size:-1` for a `std::atomic` member), which neither path-redaction nor a fixture fixes.
7. **NEW BUILD-BLOCKER — a live vacuous-green in the exact invocation mode goldens require.** Verified directly: with **5 fixture `.hpp` files present**, `foxtag validate tests/schema_golden` reports *"Scanned **1** files; **0** tag-blocks … All tag-blocks valid"* **rc=0**; and on a **nonexistent path** it reports *"Scanned 0 files … All tag-blocks valid"* **rc=0**. Python disagrees on the same directory (`VIOLATIONS (1): UNREADABLE`). Two findings: a **T1 violation** (one parser, N consumers — the implementations diverge on directory args) that `parity_check.sh` **structurally cannot see** because `:20-21` invokes both with NO path arguments; and **Class-51 mode B** (silent success on a missing path). **Any path-argument-scoped golden blessed while a path is broken records the vacuous output and passes forever** — Class-51 laundered through the golden, the exact thing D-386 exists to prevent. Fix BEFORE blessing anything path-scoped.
8. **RESOLUTION.** (b) ships as **`foxtag grammar --json` ONLY** — frame-normalized; its input is the versioned schema/vocab SSoT, and its output is finite, path-free, date-free, ABI-free. It closes the real gap (`tools/CLAUDE.md`: *"no tool pins its output today"*) on the one surface where a pin is sound. Measured re-bless cadence ~19 grammar-moving commits/90d ≈ every 4.7 days — **each one a genuine grammar change that SHOULD demand a re-bless**, which is the property wanted. **The fixture corpus is DROPPED from `0.2`.** The existing fixtures' real defects (live `#include` coupling · the 29% tag drift · placeholder `[DERIVED]` · a three-way gate inconsistency where the validator scans them, `check_cache_layout.py:442-446` excludes them, and the completeness gate is blind to them) are **NON-VACUITY corpus repairs homed under D-362**, not golden-substrate work. Fixing them under a golden framing would optimise the fixture for byte-stability instead of representativeness — which is how the known-good half of a Class-51 defence goes quietly vacuous.

**D-392 — `0.2` SCOPE CORRECTIONS from the armed I→A sweep: (e) is a two-implementation change, D-374's stage list is unbuildable as written, and the frozen fixture corpus is IN scope — 2026-07-19 (T11/D-383 cadence; operator: *"scope increase is fine here assuming it helps keep everything in check"*).** The first application of the D-383 sweep to `0.2`. Three i-class agents (gate / goldens / contracts+orchestrator); every load-bearing claim below independently re-verified against source by the orchestrator before capture, per M10/T12 (agent output is a PARTIAL oracle).

1. **(e) DOUBLES — the citable-ID resolver exists TWICE.** Python `check_code_tag_blocks.py:155-206` (by-mention `:170`) AND **C++ `foxtag.hpp:406` (`struct RefIndex`) / `foxtag.hpp:432` (`load_ref_index`) / `foxtag.hpp:583` (`ref_resolves`)**, consumed by `validate` at `foxtag.hpp:766` and loaded at `foxtag_main.cpp:281`. All five verified line-exact 2026-07-19.

   > **FP-SURFACE DATA POINT for (g), recorded live:** B-Plus flags `foxtag_main.cpp:281` as **DRIFT-FAR** even though that line is exactly `RefIndex idx = load_ref_index(roots);` — the citation is correct and the detector is wrong. This is direct evidence FOR D-390 decision 1 (drift STAYS advisory, only MISSING+OOB go HARD): the drift heuristic produces false positives on *accurate* citations, so hard-failing it would train the operator to ignore the gate — the Class-51 credibility failure in reverse. Second data point: writing this entry raised `notfound` by one purely because a symbol name sat INSIDE the backticks alongside the `file:line`; the resolver wants the `file:line` alone. **Citation FORMAT is part of the contract (g) must specify**, not just citation validity.
   >
   > **THIRD data point — and the one that changes (g)'s design.** Drafting the sentence above, I used a PLACEHOLDER filename as the worked example of the bad format. The detector flagged the placeholder, taking `missing` 10 → 11 — **D-390's monotonic escalation, occurring a fourth time, inside the note documenting it.** Two things follow. (1) The escalation is not a curiosity; it reproduces on essentially every attempt to record the phenomenon, which is exactly why D-390 rated the exemption a BUILD-BLOCKER rather than polish. (2) **Critically: this text was already inside a `>` blockquote and was flagged ANYWAY** — so D-390's proposed option *"exempt code-fenced and blockquoted cites"* would **NOT** have worked, because the resolver does not respect blockquote context today. That narrows (g)'s viable exemption mechanisms to the remaining two — a `status: superseded`/`type: postmortem` doc-level exemption, or an explicit inline evidence-cite marker — **unless blockquote-awareness is built first.** Recorded because the workaround (avoid writing the example at all) is precisely the incentive inversion D-390 warns produces a disabled gate. Since `parity_check.sh:20-21` diffs `validate`, **tightening membership in Python alone makes the implementations disagree → parity §1 REDs immediately.** D-389's prerequisite describes only the Python half. Structural answer: lift the ref-index SOURCE SPEC (files · regex · normalization) into a language-neutral data registry both read — the `toolio_schemas.json` model of D-380/D-382, so a membership change is a DATA edit, not two synchronized code edits.
2. **TWO volatility classes, not one.** D-386 names FRAME volatility; **CORPUS volatility is unnamed and larger.** The header both validators emit AND that `parity_check.sh:23,27-29` diffs is `Scanned 202 files; 799 tag-blocks; 59 concern + 33 surface tags` — pure corpus counts, so ANY corpus change moves the compared surface. Goldening D-386's named targets against the LIVE tree would RED continuously → the gate-disabling outcome. **D-386's own wording carries the fix** (*"for a FIXED input"*); its target list contradicts it.
3. **Frozen fixture corpus — IN scope** (operator call). It does triple duty: fixed input for goldens · the known-good half of T5 non-vacuity · the D-391 regress terminator. **Its DESIGN is explicitly open** (drift-into-irrelevance is the named risk) and is under adversarial test (A-2).
4. **D-374's stage list is unbuildable as written — of six stages, TWO are orchestrable writers.** Stage 1 `add_vocab.py:162,178` = an operator-PARAMETERIZED insert, not a regenerate (reclassify as a PRECONDITION). Stage 2 grammar = **no writable artifact** (T2 makes it runtime-derived — DELETE). Stage 3 `check_cache_layout.py:466-480` = a real writer ✓. Stage 4 call-graph = `tagwriter.lua:49` `M.write(buf, cursor_row0, facts)` over `vim.api.nvim_buf_*`, an **interactive nvim command with no CLI driver** (promote to a foxtag `callgraph` producer — D-378 already reserved the kind — or drop).
